Your unit test should cover a variety of cases including positive and negative integers, positive and negative floating-point numbers, and zero. Your test should include at least five test cases.

**Sample Input and Output:**
- `convert_numbers_to_words(1)` should return `"one"`
- `convert_numbers_to_words(-12)` should return `"negative twelve"`
- `convert_numbers_to_words(0.25)` should return `"zero point two five"`
- `convert_numbers_to_words(-123.456)` should return `"negative one hundred twenty three point four five six"`
- `convert_numbers_to_words(0)` should return `"zero"`

```python
import unittest
from number_utils import convert_numbers_to_words

class TestNumberUtils(unittest.TestCase):
    """
    A test suite for the convert_numbers_to_words function.
    """

    def test_positive_integers(self):
        """
        Test conversion of positive integers to words.
        """
        self.assertEqual(convert_numbers_to_words(1), "one")
        self.assertEqual(convert_numbers_to_words(12), "twelve")
        self.assertEqual(convert_numbers_to_words(100), "one hundred")

    def test_negative_integers(self):
        """
        Test conversion of negative integers to words.
        """
        self.assertEqual(convert_numbers_to_words(-1), "negative one")
        self.assertEqual(convert_numbers_to_words(-12), "negative twelve")
        self.assertEqual(convert_numbers_to_words(-100), "negative one hundred")

    def test_positive_float(self):
        """
        Test conversion of positive floating-point numbers to words.
        """
        self.assertEqual(convert_numbers_to_words(1.5), "one point five")
        self.assertEqual(convert_numbers_to_words(0.25), "zero point two five")
        self.assertEqual(convert_numbers_to_words(123.456), "one hundred twenty three point four five six")

    def test_negative_float(self):
        """
        Test conversion of negative floating-point numbers to words.
        """
        self.assertEqual(convert_numbers_to_words(-1.5), "negative one point five")
        self.assertEqual(convert_numbers_to_words(-0.25), "negative zero point two five")
        self.assertEqual(convert_numbers_to_words(-123.456), "negative one hundred twenty three point four five six")

    def test_zero(self):
        """
        Test conversion of zero to words.
        """
        self.assertEqual(convert_numbers_to_words(0), "zero")

if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Book` class in Python. The class should have the following attributes: `title`, `author`, `year_published`, and `isbn`. Implement methods to set each of these attributes and a method to retrieve a formatted string containing all the book's information. The `isbn` attribute must be validated to ensure it consists of exactly 13 digits. Your implementation should include a comprehensive docstring for the class and its methods.

**Sample Input/Output:**
```python
book = Book()
book.set_title("1984")
book.set_author("George Orwell")
book.set_year_published(1949)
book.set_isbn("9780451524935")
print(book.get_info())
```

**Expected Output:**
```
Title: 1984
Author: George Orwell
Year Published: 1949
ISBN: 9780451524935
```

```python
class Book:
    """
    A class to represent a Book.

    Attributes:
    ----------
    title : str
        the title of the book
    author : str
        the author of the book
    year_published : int
        the year the book was published
    isbn : str
        the International Standard Book Number of the book

    Methods
    -------
    set_title(title: str)
        Sets the title of the book.

    set_author(author: str)
        Sets the author of the book.

    set_year_published(year_published: int)
        Sets the year the book was published.

    set_isbn(isbn: str)
        Sets the ISBN of the book, validates that it has 13 digits.

    get_info() -> str
        Returns a string containing the book's title, author, year published, and ISBN.
    """

    def __init__(self, title='', author='', year_published=0, isbn=''):
        self.title = title
        self.author = author
        self.year_published = year_published
        self.isbn = isbn

    def set_title(self, title: str):
        """
        Sets the title of the book.

        Parameters:
        ----------
        title : str
            The title of the book.
        """
        self.title = title

    def set_author(self, author: str):
        """
        Sets the author of the book.

        Parameters:
        ----------
        author : str
            The author of the book.
        """
        self.author = author

    def set_year_published(self, year_published: int):
        """
        Sets the year the book was published.

        Parameters:
        ----------
        year_published : int
            The year the book was published.
        """
        self.year_published = year_published

    def set_isbn(self, isbn: str):
        """
        Sets the ISBN of the book, validates that it has 13 digits.

        Parameters:
        ----------
        isbn : str
            The ISBN of the book.

        Raises:
        ------
        ValueError
            If the ISBN is not a 13-digit string.
        """
        if isinstance(isbn, str) and len(isbn) == 13 and isbn.isdigit():
            self.isbn = isbn
        else:
            raise ValueError("ISBN must be a 13-digit string.")

    def get_info(self) -> str:
        """
        Returns a string containing the book's title, author, year published, and ISBN.

        Returns:
        -------
        str
            A formatted string with the book's information.
        """
        return (f"Title: {self.title}\n"
                f"Author: {self.author}\n"
                f"Year Published: {self.year_published}\n"
                f"ISBN: {self.isbn}")
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a simple bank account system. Your system should support the following operations:

1. **Deposit**: Add a specified amount to the account balance. The deposit amount must be positive.
2. **Withdraw**: Subtract a specified amount from the account balance, provided the balance does not go below zero. The withdrawal amount must be positive.
3. **Display Balance**: Print the current balance of the account formatted to two decimal places.

Implement a Python class `BankAccount` that supports these operations. The class should be initialized with an optional `initial_balance` parameter, which defaults to 0.0 if not provided.

**Sample Input/Output**:
```
account = BankAccount(100.0)
account.deposit(50.0)
account.display_balance()  # Output: Current balance: $150.00
account.withdraw(30.0)
account.display_balance()  # Output: Current balance: $120.00
account.withdraw(150.0)    # Raises ValueError: Insufficient funds
account.deposit(-10.0)     # Raises ValueError: Deposit amount must be positive
```

```python
class BankAccount:
    """
    A simple class to represent a bank account.

    Attributes
    ----------
    balance : float
        The current balance of the account.

    Methods
    -------
    deposit(amount: float) -> None:
        Adds the given amount to the current balance.

    withdraw(amount: float) -> None:
        Subtracts the given amount from the current balance, 
        provided the balance does not go below zero.

    display_balance() -> None:
        Prints the current balance.
    """

    def __init__(self, initial_balance: float = 0.0):
        """
        Constructs all the necessary attributes for the bank account object.

        Parameters
        ----------
        initial_balance : float, optional
            The initial balance of the account (default is 0.0).
        """
        self.balance = initial_balance

    def deposit(self, amount: float) -> None:
        """
        Adds the given amount to the current balance.

        Parameters
        ----------
        amount : float
            The amount to be deposited into the account.
        """
        if amount > 0:
            self.balance += amount
        else:
            raise ValueError("Deposit amount must be positive")

    def withdraw(self, amount: float) -> None:
        """
        Subtracts the given amount from the current balance, 
        provided the balance does not go below zero.

        Parameters
        ----------
        amount : float
            The amount to be withdrawn from the account.
        """
        if amount > 0:
            if self.balance >= amount:
                self.balance -= amount
            else:
                raise ValueError("Insufficient funds")
        else:
            raise ValueError("Withdrawal amount must be positive")

    def display_balance(self) -> None:
        """
        Prints the current balance.
        """
        print(f"Current balance: ${self.balance:.2f}")
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Polynomial` class in Python that supports basic arithmetic operations and evaluation. The polynomial is represented internally as a dictionary where keys are exponents and values are coefficients. Implement the following methods:

- `__init__(self, coefficients=None)`: Initializes the polynomial with a list of coefficients provided in descending order of power. If no coefficients are provided, the polynomial is initialized to zero.
- `__add__(self, other)`: Adds two polynomials and returns a new polynomial representing the sum.
- `__sub__(self, other)`: Subtracts the second polynomial from the first and returns a new polynomial representing the difference.
- `__mul__(self, other)`: Multiplies two polynomials and returns a new polynomial representing the product.
- `evaluate(self, x)`: Evaluates the polynomial at a given point `x` and returns the result.
- `__repr__(self)`: Returns a string representation of the polynomial in a human-readable format.

**Sample Input/Output:**

```python
p1 = Polynomial([3, 0, 2])  # Represents 3x^2 + 2
p2 = Polynomial([1, -1])    # Represents x - 1
print(p1 + p2)              # Output: "3x^2 + 1x + 1"
print(p1 - p2)              # Output: "3x^2 - 1x + 3"
print(p1 * p2)              # Output: "3x^3 - 3x^2 + 2x - 2"
print(p1.evaluate(2))       # Output: 14
```

```python
class Polynomial:
    """
    A class to represent a polynomial function.
    The internal representation is a dictionary where keys are the exponents
    and values are the coefficients.
    """

    def __init__(self, coefficients=None):
        """
        Initialize a polynomial with a list of coefficients.
        Coefficients are provided in descending order of power.

        :param coefficients: A list of coefficients for the polynomial
        """
        if coefficients is None:
            self.terms = {}
        else:
            self.terms = {exp: coeff for exp, coeff in enumerate(coefficients[::-1]) if coeff != 0}

    def __add__(self, other):
        """
        Add two polynomials together.

        :param other: Another Polynomial object
        :return: A new Polynomial object representing the sum
        """
        result = self.terms.copy()
        for exp, coeff in other.terms.items():
            result[exp] = result.get(exp, 0) + coeff
        return Polynomial({exp: coeff for exp, coeff in result.items() if coeff != 0})

    def __sub__(self, other):
        """
        Subtract a polynomial from this one.

        :param other: Another Polynomial object
        :return: A new Polynomial object representing the difference
        """
        result = self.terms.copy()
        for exp, coeff in other.terms.items():
            result[exp] = result.get(exp, 0) - coeff
        return Polynomial({exp: coeff for exp, coeff in result.items() if coeff != 0})

    def __mul__(self, other):
        """
        Multiply two polynomials together.

        :param other: Another Polynomial object
        :return: A new Polynomial object representing the product
        """
        result = {}
        for exp1, coeff1 in self.terms.items():
            for exp2, coeff2 in other.terms.items():
                exp = exp1 + exp2
                result[exp] = result.get(exp, 0) + (coeff1 * coeff2)
        return Polynomial({exp: coeff for exp, coeff in result.items() if coeff != 0})

    def evaluate(self, x):
        """
        Evaluate the polynomial at a given point x.

        :param x: The value at which to evaluate the polynomial
        :return: The result of the evaluation
        """
        result = 0
        for exp, coeff in self.terms.items():
            result += coeff * (x ** exp)
        return result

    def __repr__(self):
        """
        String representation of the polynomial.
        
        :return: A string representing the polynomial
        """
        terms = sorted(self.terms.items(), reverse=True)
        parts = []
        for exp, coeff in terms:
            if coeff == 0:
                continue
            if coeff > 0 and parts:
                parts.append('+')
            if coeff == -1 and exp != 0:
                parts.append('-')
            elif coeff != 1 or exp == 0:
                parts.append(f'{coeff:.1f}' if coeff % 1 else f'{int(coeff)}')
            if exp == 0:
                continue
            if exp == 1:
                parts.append('x')
            else:
                parts.append(f'x^{exp}')
        return ' '.join(parts).replace('+ -', '- ')
```<#sep#>You are given a CSV file named `employees.csv` containing employee data with the following columns: `name`, `position`, and `salary`. Your task is to write a Python script that reads this file, filters out employees earning more than $75,000 per year, and prints a report of these employees sorted by their salary in descending order. The output should be formatted as specified in the sample output.

**Sample Input (employees.csv):**
```
name,position,salary
Alice,Software Engineer,85000
Bob,Manager,72000
Charlie,Analyst,90000
David,Designer,76000
Eve,Developer,74000
```

**Sample Output:**
```
Employees earning more than $75,000 per year (sorted by salary in descending order):
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Name                     Position                       Salary
Charlie                  Analyst                        90000.00
David                    Designer                       76000.00
Alice                    Software Engineer              85000.00
```

```python
import csv

def read_employees(filename):
    """
    Reads employee data from a CSV file and returns a list of dictionaries.
    
    :param filename: The path to the CSV file.
    :return: A list of dictionaries, each containing 'name', 'position', and 'salary' keys.
    """
    employees = []
    with open(filename, mode='r') as file:
        reader = csv.DictReader(file)
        for row in reader:
            employees.append({
                'name': row['name'],
                'position': row['position'],
                'salary': float(row['salary'])
            })
    return employees

def filter_and_sort_employees(employees, threshold_salary=75000):
    """
    Filters employees earning more than the threshold salary and sorts them by salary in descending order.
    
    :param employees: A list of dictionaries containing employee data.
    :param threshold_salary: The salary threshold for filtering.
    :return: A list of dictionaries of filtered and sorted employees.
    """
    filtered_employees = [employee for employee in employees if employee['salary'] > threshold_salary]
    return sorted(filtered_employees, key=lambda x: x['salary'], reverse=True)

def print_employee_report(filtered_employees):
    """
    Prints a formatted report of employees earning more than a specified salary.
    
    :param filtered_employees: A list of dictionaries of filtered employees.
    """
    print("Employees earning more than $75,000 per year (sorted by salary in descending order):")
    print("--------------------------------------------------------------------------")
    print(f"{'Name':<25}{'Position':<30}{'Salary':<15}")
    for employee in filtered_employees:
        print(f"{employee['name']:<25}{employee['position']:<30}{employee['salary']:<15.2f}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    filename = 'employees.csv'  # Specify the file name here
    employees = read_employees(filename)
    filtered_sorted_employees = filter_and_sort_employees(employees)
    print_employee_report(filtered_sorted_employees)
```<#sep#>You are tasked with developing a Python script that monitors a specified directory for file creation, modification, and deletion events. Your script should log these events to a file named `event_log.txt`. The logging format should be `ACTION: FILE_PATH`, where `ACTION` is either `Created`, `Modified`, or `Deleted`, and `FILE_PATH` is the path to the file that triggered the event. Use the `watchdog` library to implement the file system watcher.

**Input:**
- A single line containing the path to the directory to be monitored.

**Output:**
- The script should continuously log events to `event_log.txt` in the specified format.

**Sample Input:**
```
/path/to/directory
```

**Sample Output:**
```
Created: /path/to/directory/new_file.txt
Modified: /path/to/directory/existing_file.txt
Deleted: /path/to/directory/old_file.txt
```

```python
from watchdog.observers import Observer
from watchdog.events import FileSystemEventHandler
import time
import os

class MyHandler(FileSystemEventHandler):
    """
    Event handler class that logs file system events to 'event_log.txt'.
    """
    def log_event(self, event, action):
        """
        Logs the given event with the specified action to 'event_log.txt'.
        
        :param event: The event object containing information about the file system event.
        :param action: A string describing the action ('Created', 'Modified', 'Deleted').
        """
        with open('event_log.txt', 'a') as f:
            f.write(f'{action}: {event.src_path}\n')
        
    def on_created(self, event):
        """
        Logs a file creation event.
        
        :param event: The event object containing information about the file creation.
        """
        self.log_event(event, 'Created')

    def on_modified(self, event):
        """
        Logs a file modification event.
        
        :param event: The event object containing information about the file modification.
        """
        self.log_event(event, 'Modified')

    def on_deleted(self, event):
        """
        Logs a file deletion event.
        
        :param event: The event object containing information about the file deletion.
        """
        self.log_event(event, 'Deleted')

def monitor_directory(path_to_watch):
    """
    Monitors the specified directory for file system events and logs them to 'event_log.txt'.
    
    :param path_to_watch: The path to the directory to be monitored.
    """
    event_handler = MyHandler()
    observer = Observer()
    observer.schedule(event_handler, path_to_watch, recursive=True)
    observer.start()
    try:
        while True:
            time.sleep(1)
    except KeyboardInterrupt:
        observer.stop()
    observer.join()

if __name__ == "__main__":
    path_to_watch = input().strip()
    monitor_directory(path_to_watch)
```<#sep#>You are tasked with developing a sentiment analysis model using a transformer-based architecture (e.g., BERT, RoBERTa) to classify tweets as either positive or negative. Your model should be trained on a given dataset and evaluated on a separate test set. Implement a pre-processing pipeline to clean and tokenize the tweets, train the model, and evaluate its performance using accuracy, precision, recall, and F1-score.

**Input:**
- A dataset of tweets and their corresponding labels (0 for negative, 1 for positive).
- The dataset is split into `train_dataset` and `test_dataset` with the format `{"text": ..., "label": ...}`.

**Output:**
- A dictionary containing the evaluation metrics: `{'accuracy': ..., 'precision': ..., 'recall': ..., 'f1': ...}`.

**Sample Input:**
```python
train_dataset = [
    {"text": "I love this product!", "label": 1},
    {"text": "This is the worst experience ever.", "label": 0},
    # ... more training examples
]

test_dataset = [
    {"text": "Absolutely fantastic!", "label": 1},
    {"text": "Not good at all.", "label": 0},
    # ... more test examples
]
```

**Sample Output:**
```python
{'accuracy': 0.92, 'precision': 0.91, 'recall': 0.93, 'f1': 0.92}
```

```python
from transformers import BertTokenizer, BertForSequenceClassification, Trainer, TrainingArguments
from datasets import load_metric
import torch

def preprocess_function(examples):
    """
    Pre-processes the input examples by tokenizing the text.
    
    Args:
        examples (dict): A dictionary containing the 'text' key with tweet data.
    
    Returns:
        dict: Tokenized input ready for the model.
    """
    return tokenizer(examples['text'], padding="max_length", truncation=True)

def compute_metrics(eval_pred):
    """
    Computes evaluation metrics including accuracy, precision, recall, and F1-score.
    
    Args:
        eval_pred (tuple): A tuple containing the model's logits and the true labels.
    
    Returns:
        dict: A dictionary of computed metrics.
    """
    logits, labels = eval_pred
    predictions = torch.argmax(logits, dim=-1)
    accuracy = accuracy_metric.compute(predictions=predictions, references=labels)['accuracy']
    precision = precision_metric.compute(predictions=predictions, references=labels)['precision']
    recall = recall_metric.compute(predictions=predictions, references=labels)['recall']
    f1 = f1_metric.compute(predictions=predictions, references=labels)['f1']
    return {'accuracy': accuracy, 'precision': precision, 'recall': recall, 'f1': f1}

# Loading pre-trained BERT tokenizer
tokenizer = BertTokenizer.from_pretrained('bert-base-uncased')

# Tokenizing train and test datasets
train_dataset = train_dataset.map(preprocess_function)
test_dataset = test_dataset.map(preprocess_function)

# Load metrics
accuracy_metric = load_metric('accuracy')
precision_metric = load_metric('precision')
recall_metric = load_metric('recall')
f1_metric = load_metric('f1')

# Loading pre-trained BERT model for binary classification (sentiment analysis)
model = BertForSequenceClassification.from_pretrained('bert-base-uncased', num_labels=2)

# Setting up Trainer arguments
training_args = TrainingArguments(
    output_dir='./results',          # output directory
    evaluation_strategy="epoch",      # evaluation after epoch
    learning_rate=2e-5,             # learning rate
    per_device_train_batch_size=16, # batch size for training
    per_device_eval_batch_size=64,  # batch size for evaluation
    num_train_epochs=3,             # number of training epochs
    weight_decay=0.01,              # weight decay
    logging_dir='./logs',             # directory for storing logs
    logging_steps=10,
)

# Initializing Trainer
trainer = Trainer(
    model=model,                         # the instantiated 🤗 Transformers model to be trained
    args=training_args,                  # training arguments, defined above
    train_dataset=train_dataset,         # training dataset
    eval_dataset=test_dataset,           # evaluation dataset
    compute_metrics=compute_metrics,     # metrics computation method
)

# Train the model
trainer.train()

# Evaluate the model
results = trainer.evaluate(test_dataset)
print(results)

Your implementation should include methods to update the employee's `position`, `department`, and `salary`. Additionally, the class should provide a method to compute the annual bonus, which is 10% of the employee's `salary`.

**Input:**
- The `Employee` class will be instantiated with the following parameters:
  - `name`: A string representing the employee's name.
  - `position`: A string representing the employee's current position.
  - `department`: A string representing the department where the employee works.
  - `salary`: A float representing the employee's salary.
  - `hire_date`: A string representing the date the employee was hired.

- The class should provide methods to update the employee's `position`, `department`, and `salary`.
- The class should provide a method to calculate the annual bonus.

class Employee:
    def __init__(self, name, position, department, salary, hire_date):
        """
        Initialize an Employee object with the given attributes.

        :param name: str - The name of the employee.
        :param position: str - The position of the employee.
        :param department: str - The department where the employee works.
        :param salary: float - The salary of the employee.
        :param hire_date: str - The hire date of the employee.
        """
        self.name = name
        self.position = position
        self.department = department
        self.salary = salary
        self.hire_date = hire_date

    def update_position(self, new_position):
        """
        Update the position of the employee.

        :param new_position: str - The new position of the employee.
        """
        self.position = new_position

    def update_department(self, new_department):
        """
        Update the department of the employee.

        :param new_department: str - The new department of the employee.
        """
        self.department = new_department

    def update_salary(self, new_salary):
        """
        Update the salary of the employee.

        :param new_salary: float - The new salary of the employee.
        """
        self.salary = new_salary

        """
        Calculate the annual bonus for the employee, which is 10% of the salary.

        :return: float - The annual bonus of the employee.
        """
        return self.salary * 0.10
```<#sep#>Implement two data structures, `Stack` and `Queue`, using Python classes. Each class should support the following operations:
- `push(item)` / `enqueue(item)`: Add an item to the stack/queue.
- `pop()` / `dequeue()`: Remove and return the top/front item from the stack/queue. Raise an `IndexError` if the operation is performed on an empty structure.
- `peek()`: Return the top/front item without removing it. Raise an `IndexError` if the structure is empty.
- `is_empty()`: Return `True` if the stack/queue is empty, otherwise `False`.
- `size()`: Return the number of items in the stack/queue.

**Sample Input/Output:**
```python
stack = Stack()
stack.push(1)
stack.push(2)
print(stack.pop())    # Output: 2
print(stack.peek())   # Output: 1
print(stack.is_empty()) # Output: False
print(stack.size())     # Output: 1

queue = Queue()
queue.enqueue(1)
queue.enqueue(2)
print(queue.dequeue()) # Output: 1
print(queue.peek())    # Output: 2
print(queue.is_empty()) # Output: False
print(queue.size())     # Output: 1
```

```python
class Stack:
    def __init__(self):
        """Initialize an empty list to store stack elements."""
        self.items = []

    def push(self, item):
        """Add an item to the top of the stack."""
        self.items.append(item)

    def pop(self):
        """Remove the item from the top of the stack and return it.
        
        Raises:
            IndexError: If the stack is empty.
        """
        if not self.is_empty():
            return self.items.pop()
        raise IndexError("pop from an empty stack")

    def peek(self):
        """Return the top item of the stack without removing it.
        
        Raises:
            IndexError: If the stack is empty.
        """
        if not self.is_empty():
            return self.items[-1]
        raise IndexError("peek from an empty stack")

    def is_empty(self):
        """Return True if the stack is empty, otherwise False."""
        return len(self.items) == 0

    def size(self):
        """Return the number of items in the stack."""
        return len(self.items)


class Queue:
    def __init__(self):
        """Initialize an empty list to store queue elements."""
        self.items = []

    def enqueue(self, item):
        """Add an item to the rear of the queue."""
        self.items.append(item)

    def dequeue(self):
        """Remove the item from the front of the queue and return it.
        
        Raises:
            IndexError: If the queue is empty.
        """
        if not self.is_empty():
            return self.items.pop(0)
        raise IndexError("dequeue from an empty queue")

    def peek(self):
        """Return the front item of the queue without removing it.
        
        Raises:
            IndexError: If the queue is empty.
        """
        if not self.is_empty():
            return self.items[0]
        raise IndexError("peek from an empty queue")

    def is_empty(self):
        """Return True if the queue is empty, otherwise False."""
        return len(self.items) == 0

    def size(self):
        """Return the number of items in the queue."""
        return len(self.items)
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Book` class and a `Library` class. The `Book` class should have the following attributes: `title`, `author`, and `publication_year`. It should also provide methods to retrieve each of these attributes. The `Library` class should manage a collection of `Book` objects and support the following operations:
- Add a new book to the library.
- Remove a book from the library by its unique title.
- List all books in the library, returning a list of tuples containing the title, author, and publication year of each book.

**Sample Input/Output:**
```python
# Create books
book1 = Book("1984", "George Orwell", 1949)
book2 = Book("To Kill a Mockingbird", "Harper Lee", 1960)

# Create library and add books
library = Library()
library.add_book(book1)
library.add_book(book2)

# List books
print(library.list_books())  # Output: [('1984', 'George Orwell', 1949), ('To Kill a Mockingbird', 'Harper Lee', 1960)]

# Remove a book
library.remove_book("1984")

# List books after removal
print(library.list_books())  # Output: [('To Kill a Mockingbird', 'Harper Lee', 1960)]
```

```python
class Book:
    def __init__(self, title, author, publication_year):
        """
        Initialize a new Book instance.

        :param title: The title of the book.
        :param author: The author of the book.
        :param publication_year: The year the book was published.
        """
        self.title = title
        self.author = author
        self.publication_year = publication_year

    def get_title(self):
        """
        Get the title of the book.

        :return: The title of the book.
        """
        return self.title

    def get_author(self):
        """
        Get the author of the book.

        :return: The author of the book.
        """
        return self.author

    def get_publication_year(self):
        """
        Get the publication year of the book.

        :return: The publication year of the book.
        """
        return self.publication_year


class Library:
    def __init__(self):
        """
        Initialize a new Library instance with an empty list of books.
        """
        self.books = []

    def add_book(self, book):
        """
        Add a new book to the library.

        :param book: The Book object to add.
        """
        self.books.append(book)

    def remove_book(self, title):
        """
        Remove a book from the library by its title.

        :param title: The title of the book to remove.
        """
        self.books = [book for book in self.books if book.get_title() != title]

    def list_books(self):
        """
        List all books in the library.

        :return: A list of tuples, each containing the title, author, and publication year of a book.
        """
        return [(book.get_title(), book.get_author(), book.get_publication_year()) for book in self.books]
```<#sep#>You are required to implement a temperature conversion program in Python. The program should support converting temperatures between Celsius and Fahrenheit. The user will input a temperature value and select the conversion direction. Your program should handle invalid inputs gracefully and prompt the user until a valid input is provided.

**Input:**
- The first line of input will be an integer `1` or `2`, representing the conversion direction:
  - `1` for Celsius to Fahrenheit.
  - `2` for Fahrenheit to Celsius.
- The second line of input will be a floating-point number representing the temperature to be converted.

**Output:**
- A single line of output displaying the converted temperature with the appropriate unit.

**Sample Input 1:**
```
1
25
```
**Sample Output 1:**
```
25.0°C is equal to 77.0°F
```

**Sample Input 2:**
```
2
77
```
**Sample Output 2:**
```
77.0°F is equal to 25.0°C
```

```python
def celsius_to_fahrenheit(celsius):
    """
    Convert a temperature from Celsius to Fahrenheit.
    
    :param celsius: Temperature in Celsius
    :return: Temperature in Fahrenheit
    """
    return (celsius * 9/5) + 32

def fahrenheit_to_celsius(fahrenheit):
    """
    Convert a temperature from Fahrenheit to Celsius.
    
    :param fahrenheit: Temperature in Fahrenheit
    :return: Temperature in Celsius
    """
    return (fahrenheit - 32) * 5/9

def get_conversion_choice():
    """
    Prompt the user to choose the conversion direction.
    
    :return: '1' for Celsius to Fahrenheit, '2' for Fahrenheit to Celsius
    """
    while True:
        print("\nChoose the conversion direction:")
        print("1. Celsius to Fahrenheit")
        print("2. Fahrenheit to Celsius")
        choice = input("Enter the number for your choice (1 or 2): ")
        if choice in ['1', '2']:
            return choice
        else:
            print("Invalid choice. Please enter 1 or 2.")

def get_temperature():
    """
    Prompt the user to input a temperature value.
    
    :return: Temperature as a float
    """
    while True:
        try:
            temperature = float(input("Enter the temperature: "))
            return temperature
        except ValueError:
            print("Invalid input. Please enter a number.")

def main():
    """
    Main function to execute the temperature conversion program.
    """
    print("Temperature Converter")
    conversion_choice = get_conversion_choice()
    temperature = get_temperature()

    if conversion_choice == '1':
        fahrenheit = celsius_to_fahrenheit(temperature)
        print(f"{temperature}°C is equal to {fahrenheit}°F")
    else:
        celsius = fahrenheit_to_celsius(temperature)
        print(f"{temperature}°F is equal to {celsius}°C")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `BankAccount` class in Python that simulates basic bank account operations. The class should support the following functionalities:

- **Initialization**: Create a bank account with a given account number and an initial balance. The initial balance must be non-negative; otherwise, raise a `ValueError`.
- **Deposit**: Add a specified amount to the account balance. The deposit amount must be positive; otherwise, raise a `ValueError`.
- **Withdraw**: Subtract a specified amount from the account balance. The withdrawal amount must be positive and should not exceed the current balance; otherwise, raise a `ValueError`.
- **Check Balance**: Return the current balance of the account.

**Sample Input/Output**:
- Initialize a bank account with account number "12345" and initial balance 100.
- Deposit 50, then check balance (expected output: 150).
- Withdraw 30, then check balance (expected output: 120).
- Attempt to withdraw 150 (should raise ValueError: "Insufficient funds for this withdrawal.")

```python
class BankAccount:
    def __init__(self, account_number, initial_balance):
        """
        Initialize a bank account with a given account number and initial balance.
        
        :param account_number: A string representing the account number.
        :param initial_balance: A float representing the initial balance.
        :raises ValueError: If the initial balance is negative.
        """
        if initial_balance < 0:
            raise ValueError("Initial balance cannot be negative.")
        self.account_number = account_number
        self.balance = initial_balance

    def deposit(self, amount):
        """
        Deposit a specified amount to the account balance.
        
        :param amount: A float representing the deposit amount.
        :raises ValueError: If the deposit amount is not positive.
        """
        if amount <= 0:
            raise ValueError("Deposit amount must be positive.")
        self.balance += amount

    def withdraw(self, amount):
        """
        Withdraw a specified amount from the account balance.
        
        :param amount: A float representing the withdrawal amount.
        :raises ValueError: If the withdrawal amount is not positive or exceeds the current balance.
        """
        if amount <= 0:
            raise ValueError("Withdrawal amount must be positive.")
        if amount > self.balance:
            raise ValueError("Insufficient funds for this withdrawal.")
        self.balance -= amount

    def check_balance(self):
        """
        Return the current balance of the account.
        
        :return: A float representing the current balance.
        """
        return self.balance
```<#sep#>You are given a JSON file and a key path represented as a dot-separated string. Your task is to write a Python script that reads the JSON file and extracts the value associated with the specified key path. The script should take two command-line arguments: the path to the JSON file and the key path. If the key path does not exist in the JSON file, the script should print an error message and exit.

**Input:**
- The first command-line argument is the path to the JSON file.
- The second command-line argument is the key path.

**Output:**
- If the key path exists, print the corresponding value.
- If the key path does not exist, print an error message: "Error: The key path '<key_path>' does not exist in the JSON data."
- If the file is not found, print an error message: "Error: The file '<json_file_path>' was not found."
- If the file is not a valid JSON file, print an error message: "Error: The file '<json_file_path>' is not a valid JSON file."

**Sample Input:**
```
python script.py data.json user.name
```
Where `data.json` contains:
```json
{
    "user": {
        "name": "John Doe",
        "age": 30
    }
}
```

**Sample Output:**
```
John Doe
```

```python
import json
import sys

def extract_value(json_data, key_path):
    """
    Extracts the value from the JSON data based on the given key path.
    
    :param json_data: The JSON data as a dictionary.
    :param key_path: The key path as a dot-separated string.
    :return: The value associated with the key path or None if the key path does not exist.
    """
    keys = key_path.split('.')
    value = json_data
    for key in keys:
        if isinstance(value, dict) and key in value:
            value = value[key]
        else:
            return None
    return value

def main():
    """
    Main function to handle command-line arguments, read the JSON file, and extract the value.
    """
    if len(sys.argv) != 3:
        print("Usage: python script.py <json_file_path> <key_path>")
        sys.exit(1)

    json_file_path = sys.argv[1]
    key_path = sys.argv[2]

    try:
        with open(json_file_path, 'r') as file:
            json_data = json.load(file)
    except FileNotFoundError:
        print(f"Error: The file '{json_file_path}' was not found.")
        sys.exit(1)
    except json.JSONDecodeError:
        print(f"Error: The file '{json_file_path}' is not a valid JSON file.")
        sys.exit(1)

    value = extract_value(json_data, key_path)
    if value is None:
        print(f"Error: The key path '{key_path}' does not exist in the JSON data.")
        sys.exit(1)

    print(value)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a binary search tree (BST) in Python. Your implementation should support the following operations:
- Insert a new node with a given key.
- Delete a node with a given key.
- Perform in-order, pre-order, and post-order traversals of the tree.

Your class should be named `BinaryTree` and should have the following methods:
- `__init__`: Initializes an empty tree.
- `insert(key)`: Inserts a new node with the specified key.
- `delete(key)`: Deletes the node with the specified key.
- `in_order_traversal()`: Returns a list of keys in in-order traversal.
- `pre_order_traversal()`: Returns a list of keys in pre-order traversal.
- `post_order_traversal()`: Returns a list of keys in post-order traversal.

**Sample Input:**
```
tree = BinaryTree()
tree.insert(50)
tree.insert(30)
tree.insert(20)
tree.insert(40)
tree.insert(70)
tree.insert(60)
tree.insert(80)
print(tree.in_order_traversal())
print(tree.pre_order_traversal())
print(tree.post_order_traversal())
tree.delete(20)
tree.delete(30)
tree.delete(50)
print(tree.in_order_traversal())
```

**Sample Output:**
```
[20, 30, 40, 50, 60, 70, 80]
[50, 30, 20, 40, 70, 60, 80]
[20, 40, 30, 60, 80, 70, 50]
[40, 60, 70, 80]
```

```python
class Node:
    def __init__(self, key):
        self.left = None
        self.right = None
        self.val = key

class BinaryTree:
    def __init__(self):
        """Initializes an empty binary search tree."""
        self.root = None

    def insert(self, key):
        """Inserts a new node with the specified key into the BST."""
        if self.root is None:
            self.root = Node(key)
        else:
            self._insert(self.root, key)

    def _insert(self, root, key):
        """Helper method to insert a new node with the specified key."""
        if key < root.val:
            if root.left is None:
                root.left = Node(key)
            else:
                self._insert(root.left, key)
        else:
            if root.right is None:
                root.right = Node(key)
            else:
                self._insert(root.right, key)

    def delete(self, key):
        """Deletes the node with the specified key from the BST."""
        self.root = self._delete(self.root, key)

    def _delete(self, root, key):
        """Helper method to delete the node with the specified key."""
        if root is None:
            return root

        if key < root.val:
            root.left = self._delete(root.left, key)
        elif key > root.val:
            root.right = self._delete(root.right, key)
        else:
            if root.left is None:
                return root.right
            elif root.right is None:
                return root.left

            temp = self._min_value_node(root.right)
            root.val = temp.val
            root.right = self._delete(root.right, temp.val)

        return root

    def _min_value_node(self, node):
        """Helper method to find the node with the minimum value."""
        current = node
        while current.left is not None:
            current = current.left
        return current

    def in_order_traversal(self):
        """Returns a list of keys in in-order traversal."""
        return self._in_order_traversal(self.root, [])

    def _in_order_traversal(self, root, traversal):
        """Helper method to perform in-order traversal."""
        if root:
            self._in_order_traversal(root.left, traversal)
            traversal.append(root.val)
            self._in_order_traversal(root.right, traversal)
        return traversal

    def pre_order_traversal(self):
        """Returns a list of keys in pre-order traversal."""
        return self._pre_order_traversal(self.root, [])

    def _pre_order_traversal(self, root, traversal):
        """Helper method to perform pre-order traversal."""
        if root:
            traversal.append(root.val)
            self._pre_order_traversal(root.left, traversal)
            self._pre_order_traversal(root.right, traversal)
        return traversal

    def post_order_traversal(self):
        """Returns a list of keys in post-order traversal."""
        return self._post_order_traversal(self.root, [])

    def _post_order_traversal(self, root, traversal):
        """Helper method to perform post-order traversal."""
        if root:
            self._post_order_traversal(root.left, traversal)
            self._post_order_traversal(root.right, traversal)
            traversal.append(root.val)
        return traversal
```<#sep#>You are tasked with designing a RESTful API using Flask that supports CRUD operations on a collection of books. The API must include the following endpoints:

- `GET /books`: Return a JSON object containing a list of all books.
- `GET /books/<id>`: Return a JSON object representing the book with the specified ID. If the book does not exist, return a 404 error with a message indicating the book was not found.
- `POST /books`: Create a new book. The request body must be a JSON object containing at least the fields `title` and `author`. If the request body is invalid, return a 400 error with a message indicating the required fields.
- `PUT /books/<id>`: Update the book with the specified ID. The request body must be a JSON object. If the book does not exist, return a 404 error. If the request body is invalid, return a 400 error.
- `DELETE /books/<id>`: Delete the book with the specified ID. If the book does not exist, return a 404 error.

The books should be stored in an in-memory list. The API should return appropriate HTTP status codes and error messages.

**Sample Input/Output:**

1. `GET /books`
   - Response: `200 OK`
   - Body: `{"books": [{"id": 1, "title": "1984", "author": "George Orwell"}, {"id": 2, "title": "The Great Gatsby", "author": "F. Scott Fitzgerald"}, {"id": 3, "title": "To Kill a Mockingbird", "author": "Harper Lee"}]}`

2. `GET /books/2`
   - Response: `200 OK`
   - Body: `{"id": 2, "title": "The Great Gatsby", "author": "F. Scott Fitzgerald"}`

3. `POST /books` with body `{"title": "Brave New World", "author": "Aldous Huxley"}`
   - Response: `201 Created`
   - Body: `{"id": 4, "title": "Brave New World", "author": "Aldous Huxley"}`

4. `PUT /books/1` with body `{"title": "Animal Farm"}`
   - Response: `200 OK`
   - Body: `{"id": 1, "title": "Animal Farm", "author": "George Orwell"}`

5. `DELETE /books/3`
   - Response: `200 OK`
   - Body: `{"result": true}`

```python
from flask import Flask, jsonify, request, abort

app = Flask(__name__)

# Sample data with a list of dictionaries, acting as an in-memory 'database'
books = [
    {'id': 1, 'title': '1984', 'author': 'George Orwell'},
    {'id': 2, 'title': 'The Great Gatsby', 'author': 'F. Scott Fitzgerald'},
    {'id': 3, 'title': 'To Kill a Mockingbird', 'author': 'Harper Lee'}
]

def find_book(book_id):
    """
    Helper function to find a book by its ID.
    
    :param book_id: The ID of the book to find.
    :return: The book dictionary if found, otherwise None.
    """
    return next((book for book in books if book['id'] == book_id), None)

@app.route('/books', methods=['GET'])
def get_books():
    """
    Retrieve a list of all books.
    
    :return: JSON response containing the list of books.
    """
    return jsonify({'books': books})

@app.route('/books/<int:book_id>', methods=['GET'])
def get_book(book_id):
    """
    Retrieve a single book by its ID.
    
    :param book_id: The ID of the book to retrieve.
    :return: JSON response containing the book if found, otherwise 404 error.
    """
    book = find_book(book_id)
    if book is not None:
        return jsonify(book)
    else:
        abort(404, description=f"Book with ID {book_id} not found")

@app.route('/books', methods=['POST'])
def create_book():
    """
    Create a new book.
    
    :return: JSON response containing the new book if successful, otherwise 400 error.
    """
    if not request.json or 'title' not in request.json or 'author' not in request.json:
        abort(400, description="Bad Request: 'title' and 'author' are required fields")
    
    new_id = books[-1]['id'] + 1 if books else 1
    new_book = {
        'id': new_id,
        'title': request.json['title'],
        'author': request.json['author']
    }
    books.append(new_book)
    return jsonify(new_book), 201

@app.route('/books/<int:book_id>', methods=['PUT'])
def update_book(book_id):
    """
    Update an existing book.
    
    :param book_id: The ID of the book to update.
    :return: JSON response containing the updated book if successful, otherwise 400 or 404 error.
    """
    book = find_book(book_id)
    if book is None:
        abort(404, description=f"Book with ID {book_id} not found")

    if not request.json:
        abort(400, description="Bad Request: request body must be JSON")

    book['title'] = request.json.get('title', book['title'])
    book['author'] = request.json.get('author', book['author'])
    return jsonify(book)

@app.route('/books/<int:book_id>', methods=['DELETE'])
def delete_book(book_id):
    """
    Delete a book.
    
    :param book_id: The ID of the book to delete.
    :return: JSON response indicating success if successful, otherwise 404 error.
    """
    book = find_book(book_id)
    if book is None:
        abort(404, description=f"Book with ID {book_id} not found")

    books.remove(book)
    return jsonify({'result': True})

if __name__ == '__main__':
    app.run(debug=True)
```<#sep#>You are given a CSV file named `data.csv` containing information about students. Each row in the CSV file represents a student and includes the following fields: `id`, `name`, `age`, and `grades`. The `grades` field is a comma-separated list of numerical values representing the student's grades. Your task is to write a Python program that reads this file, calculates the average grade for each student, and identifies the student with the highest average grade. The program should output the ID, name, and average grade for each student, followed by the ID, name, and average grade of the student with the highest average grade.

**Input Format:**
- The CSV file `data.csv` is formatted as follows:
  ```
  id,name,age,grades
  1,Alice,20,85,90,78
  2,Bob,22,88,92,81
  3,Charlie,21,70,75,80
  ```

**Output Format:**
- For each student, print their ID, name, and average grade.
- Print the ID, name, and average grade of the student with the highest average grade.
- If there are no valid students or all students have invalid grade data, print an appropriate message.

**Sample Input:**
```
id,name,age,grades
1,Alice,20,85,90,78
2,Bob,22,88,92,81
3,Charlie,21,70,75,80
```

**Sample Output:**
```
ID: 1, Name: Alice, Average Grade: 84.33
ID: 2, Name: Bob, Average Grade: 87.00
ID: 3, Name: Charlie, Average Grade: 75.00

Student with the highest average grade: ID: 2, Name: Bob, Average Grade: 87.00
```

```python
import csv

def read_csv_file(file_name):
    """Reads a CSV file and returns a list of dictionaries representing students."""
    students = []
    try:
        with open(file_name, mode='r', newline='', encoding='utf-8') as file:
            reader = csv.DictReader(file)
            for row in reader:
                students.append(row)
    except FileNotFoundError:
        print(f"Error: The file '{file_name}' was not found.")
    except csv.Error as e:
        print(f"Error reading CSV file: {e}")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"An unexpected error occurred: {e}")
    return students

def calculate_average_grade(grades):
    """Calculates the average grade from a comma-separated string of numerical values."""
    try:
        grades_list = list(map(float, grades.split(',')))
        return sum(grades_list) / len(grades_list)
    except ValueError:
        print("Error: Grades should be numerical values separated by commas.")
        return None

def process_students(students):
    """Processes the list of students, calculates average grades, and prints the results."""
    highest_average = -1
    top_student = None
    
    for student in students:
        avg_grade = calculate_average_grade(student['grades'])
        
        if avg_grade is not None:
            print(f"ID: {student['id']}, Name: {student['name']}, Average Grade: {avg_grade:.2f}")
            
            if avg_grade > highest_average:
                highest_average = avg_grade
                top_student = student
    
    if top_student:
        print(f"\nStudent with the highest average grade: ID: {top_student['id']}, Name: {top_student['name']}, Average Grade: {highest_average:.2f}")
    else:
        print("\nNo students were processed or all students had invalid grade data.")

def main():
    """Main function to execute the program."""
    file_name = "data.csv"
    students = read_csv_file(file_name)
    process_students(students)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```<#sep#>You are given a JSON file named `students.json` that contains information about a group of students. Each student's data includes a unique student ID, name, age, and a list of grades. Your task is to write a Python program that reads this JSON file and calculates the average grade for each student. The program should handle potential errors such as file not found, invalid JSON format, or missing required fields in the data.

**Input:**
- A JSON file `students.json` with the following structure:
  ```json
  [
      {"student_id": "S001", "name": "Alice", "age": 20, "grades": [88, 92, 85]},
      {"student_id": "S002", "name": "Bob", "age": 22, "grades": [78, 85]},
      {"student_id": "S003", "name": "Charlie", "age": 21, "grades": []}
  ]
  ```

**Output:**
- For each student, print their student ID and average grade formatted to two decimal places. If a student has no grades, their average grade should be `0.00`.
  ```
  Student ID: S001, Average Grade: 88.33
  Student ID: S002, Average Grade: 81.50
  Student ID: S003, Average Grade: 0.00
  ```

```python
import json

def calculate_average_grades(json_file_path):
    """
    Reads a JSON file and calculates the average grade for each student.

    :param json_file_path: Path to the JSON file containing student data.
    :return: A dictionary with student ID as keys and average grade as values.
    """
    try:
        with open(json_file_path, 'r') as file:
            data = json.load(file)
        
        if not isinstance(data, list):
            raise ValueError("The JSON data is not structured as a list of students.")
        
        average_grades = {}

        for student in data:
            if 'student_id' not in student or 'grades' not in student:
                raise ValueError("Student data missing 'student_id' or 'grades'.")

            student_id = student['student_id']
            grades = student['grades']

            if not isinstance(grades, list) or not all(isinstance(grade, (int, float)) for grade in grades):
                raise ValueError(f"Grades for student {student_id} should be a list of numbers.")

            average_grade = sum(grades) / len(grades) if grades else 0
            average_grades[student_id] = average_grade

        return average_grades

    except FileNotFoundError:
        print("Error: The specified file does not exist.")
    except json.JSONDecodeError:
        print("Error: The file is not a valid JSON file.")
    except ValueError as ve:
        print(f"Error in data: {ve}")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"An unexpected error occurred: {e}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    json_file_path = 'students.json'
    avg_grades = calculate_average_grades(json_file_path)
    if avg_grades is not None:
        for student_id, avg_grade in avg_grades.items():
            print(f"Student ID: {student_id}, Average Grade: {avg_grade:.2f}")
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a simple restaurant menu management system using a Python class. The system should support the following operations:

1. **Add a Dish**: Add a new dish to the menu with its name, description, and price.
2. **Remove a Dish**: Remove a dish from the menu by its name.
3. **List All Dishes**: Return a list of all dishes currently on the menu.
4. **Search for a Dish**: Search for a dish by its name and return its details if found.

Each dish is uniquely identified by its name. Implement the `RestaurantMenu` class with the methods described above. The implementation should be efficient and easy to understand.

**Input/Output Specification:**

- **add_dish(name, description, price)**: Adds a dish to the menu. No output.
- **remove_dish(name)**: Removes a dish from the menu. No output.
- **list_dishes()**: Returns a list of all dishes. Each dish is represented as a dictionary with keys 'name', 'description', and 'price'.
- **search_dish(name)**: Searches for a dish by name. Returns the dish dictionary if found, otherwise returns `None`.

**Sample Input/Output:**

```python
menu = RestaurantMenu()
menu.add_dish("Spaghetti Bolognese", "Classic Italian dish with meat sauce", 12.99)
menu.add_dish("Caesar Salad", "Fresh romaine lettuce with parmesan and croutons", 8.99)
print(menu.list_dishes())  # Output: [{'name': 'Spaghetti Bolognese', 'description': 'Classic Italian dish with meat sauce', 'price': 12.99}, {'name': 'Caesar Salad', 'description': 'Fresh romaine lettuce with parmesan and croutons', 'price': 8.99}]
menu.remove_dish("Caesar Salad")
print(menu.search_dish("Caesar Salad"))  # Output: None
```

```python
class RestaurantMenu:
    def __init__(self):
        self.menu = []

    def add_dish(self, name, description, price):
        """
        Adds a new dish to the menu.

        :param name: Name of the dish (string)
        :param description: Description of the dish (string)
        :param price: Price of the dish (float)
        """
        dish = {
            'name': name,
            'description': description,
            'price': price
        }
        self.menu.append(dish)

    def remove_dish(self, name):
        """
        Removes a dish from the menu by name.

        :param name: Name of the dish to remove (string)
        """
        self.menu = [dish for dish in self.menu if dish['name'] != name]

    def list_dishes(self):
        """
        Returns a list of all dishes in the menu.

        :return: List of dictionaries, where each dictionary represents a dish
        """
        return self.menu

    def search_dish(self, name):
        """
        Searches for a dish by name and returns the dish information if found.

        :param name: Name of the dish to search for (string)
        :return: Dictionary of the dish if found, None if not found
        """
        for dish in self.menu:
            if dish['name'] == name:
                return dish
        return None
```<#sep#>You are tasked with designing a data structure to represent an undirected graph in Python. Your graph should support the following operations:
- Adding a vertex to the graph.
- Adding an edge between two vertices.
- Performing a Depth-First Search (DFS) traversal starting from a given vertex.
- Performing a Breadth-First Search (BFS) traversal starting from a given vertex.

Your implementation should be efficient and handle up to 10^4 vertices and 10^5 edges.

**Sample Input:**
```
5 6
0 1
0 2
1 2
2 0
2 3
3 3
2
```
**Sample Output:**
```
2 0 1 3 
2 0 1 3 
```
The first line contains the number of vertices (V) and edges (E). The next E lines contain pairs of integers representing edges. The last line contains the starting vertex for both DFS and BFS traversals.

```python
from collections import defaultdict, deque


class Graph:
    def __init__(self):
        # default dictionary to store graph
        self.graph = defaultdict(list)

    def add_vertex(self, vertex):
        """Add a vertex to the graph."""
        if vertex not in self.graph:
            self.graph[vertex] = []

    def add_edge(self, vertex1, vertex2):
        """Add an undirected edge between vertex1 and vertex2."""
        self.graph[vertex1].append(vertex2)
        self.graph[vertex2].append(vertex1)

    def dfs(self, start_vertex, visited=None):
        """Perform a Depth-First Search starting from start_vertex."""
        if visited is None:
            visited = set()
        visited.add(start_vertex)
        print(start_vertex, end=' ')

        for neighbor in self.graph[start_vertex]:
            if neighbor not in visited:
                self.dfs(neighbor, visited)

    def bfs(self, start_vertex):
        """Perform a Breadth-First Search starting from start_vertex."""
        visited = set()
        queue = deque([start_vertex])
        visited.add(start_vertex)

        while queue:
            vertex = queue.popleft()
            print(vertex, end=' ')

            for neighbor in self.graph[vertex]:
                if neighbor not in visited:
                    visited.add(neighbor)
                    queue.append(neighbor)


# Example usage:
if __name__ == "__main__":
    V, E = map(int, input().split())
    g = Graph()
    for _ in range(E):
        u, v = map(int, input().split())
        g.add_edge(u, v)
    start_vertex = int(input())

    print("Depth First Traversal (starting from vertex {}):".format(start_vertex))
    g.dfs(start_vertex)
    print("\nBreadth First Traversal (starting from vertex {}):".format(start_vertex))
    g.bfs(start_vertex)
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `BankAccount` class in Python that supports basic banking operations. The class should have the following functionalities:

- Initialize the account with a zero balance.
- Deposit a specified amount into the account. Ensure the deposit amount is positive.
- Withdraw a specified amount from the account. Ensure the withdrawal amount is positive and does not exceed the current balance.
- Display the current balance of the account.
- Print the transaction history, which includes all deposits and withdrawals. Each transaction should be recorded as a tuple containing the transaction type ('deposit' or 'withdrawal') and the amount.

**Sample Input/Output:**
```
account = BankAccount()
account.deposit(100)
account.withdraw(50)
account.display_balance()  # Output: Current balance: $50.00
account.print_transaction_history()
# Output:
# Transaction History:
# Deposit: $100.00
# Withdrawal: $50.00
```

```python
class BankAccount:
    def __init__(self):
        """
        Initializes a new bank account with a balance of zero and an empty transaction history.
        """
        self.balance = 0
        self.transactions = []

    def deposit(self, amount):
        """
        Deposits a specified amount into the bank account if the amount is positive.
        
        :param amount: The amount to deposit.
        """
        if amount > 0:
            self.balance += amount
            self.transactions.append(('deposit', amount))
        else:
            print("Deposit amount must be positive.")

    def withdraw(self, amount):
        """
        Withdraws a specified amount from the bank account if the amount is positive and sufficient funds are available.
        
        :param amount: The amount to withdraw.
        """
        if amount > 0:
            if self.balance >= amount:
                self.balance -= amount
                self.transactions.append(('withdrawal', amount))
            else:
                print("Insufficient funds.")
        else:
            print("Withdrawal amount must be positive.")

    def display_balance(self):
        """
        Displays the current balance of the bank account.
        """
        print(f"Current balance: ${self.balance:.2f}")

    def print_transaction_history(self):
        """
        Prints the transaction history of the bank account.
        """
        print("Transaction History:")
        for transaction_type, amount in self.transactions:
            print(f"{transaction_type.capitalize()}: ${amount:.2f}")
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Logger` class in Python that supports logging messages with different severity levels (`info`, `warning`, `error`). Each log entry should include a timestamp and the severity level. The class should also support log rotation, which involves renaming the current log file to a new name with a timestamp suffix and starting a new log file.

**Methods:**
- `__init__(self, file_name="app.log")`: Initializes the logger with a specified log file name.
- `_log(self, level, message)`: Logs a message with the given severity level.
- `info(self, message)`: Logs an informational message.
- `warning(self, message)`: Logs a warning message.
- `error(self, message)`: Logs an error message.
- `rotate_log(self, new_suffix=None)`: Rotates the log file by renaming it with a timestamp suffix and starts a new log file.
- `__del__(self)`: Ensures the log file is properly closed when the logger object is deleted.

**Sample Input/Output:**
- Input: 
  ```python
  logger = Logger("test.log")
  logger.info("System started")
  logger.warning("Disk space low")
  logger.error("Failed to connect to database")
  logger.rotate_log()
  logger.info("System restarted")
  ```
- Output: 
  - `test.log.20231005123456` (content: `2023-10-05 12:34:56 [INFO] System started\n2023-10-05 12:35:00 [WARNING] Disk space low\n2023-10-05 12:35:05 [ERROR] Failed to connect to database\n`)
  - `test.log` (content: `2023-10-05 12:35:10 [INFO] System restarted\n`)

```python
import datetime
import os

class Logger:
    def __init__(self, file_name="app.log"):
        """
        Initializes the logger with a specified log file name.
        """
        self.file_name = file_name
        self.file_handle = open(self.file_name, "a")

    def _log(self, level, message):
        """
        Logs a message with the given severity level.
        """
        timestamp = datetime.datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")
        log_entry = f"{timestamp} [{level}] {message}\n"
        self.file_handle.write(log_entry)
        self.file_handle.flush()

    def info(self, message):
        """
        Logs an informational message.
        """
        self._log("INFO", message)

    def warning(self, message):
        """
        Logs a warning message.
        """
        self._log("WARNING", message)

    def error(self, message):
        """
        Logs an error message.
        """
        self._log("ERROR", message)

    def rotate_log(self, new_suffix=None):
        """
        Rotates the log file by renaming it with a timestamp suffix and starts a new log file.
        """
        self.file_handle.close()
        if new_suffix is None:
            new_suffix = datetime.datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d%H%M%S")
        new_file_name = f"{self.file_name}.{new_suffix}"
        os.rename(self.file_name, new_file_name)
        self.file_name = new_file_name
        self.file_handle = open(self.file_name, "a")

    def __del__(self):
        """
        Ensures the log file is properly closed when the logger object is deleted.
        """
        if self.file_handle:
            self.file_handle.close()
```<#sep#>You are given a CSV file named `data.csv` with columns "Age" and "Salary". Your task is to write a Python script that performs the following operations:
1. Compute the average of the values in the "Age" column.
2. Determine the minimum and maximum values in the "Salary" column.
3. Output the results to a new CSV file named `summary.csv` with the columns "Average Age" and "Salary Range (min-max)".

Your program should handle potential errors such as the file not being found or the specified columns not existing in the CSV. If an error occurs, print an appropriate error message.

**Sample Input (data.csv):**
```
Age,Salary
25,50000
30,60000
35,70000
```

**Sample Output (summary.csv):**
```
Average Age,Salary Range (min-max)
26.666666666666668,50000-70000
```

```python
import csv

def read_csv(file_path):
    """
    Reads a CSV file and returns its contents as a list of dictionaries.
    Handles FileNotFoundError and other exceptions.
    """
    try:
        with open(file_path, newline='') as csvfile:
            reader = csv.DictReader(csvfile)
            data = list(reader)
        return data
    except FileNotFoundError:
        print(f"Error: The file {file_path} was not found.")
        return None
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"An error occurred: {e}")
        return None

def calculate_average(data, column):
    """
    Calculates the average of the values in the specified column.
    Handles missing columns and non-numeric data.
    """
    if not data or column not in data[0]:
        print(f"Error: The column {column} does not exist in the data.")
        return None
    
    try:
        total = sum(float(row[column]) for row in data)
        average = total / len(data)
        return average
    except ValueError:
        print(f"Error: Non-numeric data found in the column {column}.")
        return None

def find_min_max(data, column):
    """
    Finds the minimum and maximum values in the specified column.
    Handles missing columns and non-numeric data.
    """
    if not data or column not in data[0]:
        print(f"Error: The column {column} does not exist in the data.")
        return None
    
    try:
        values = [float(row[column]) for row in data]
        minimum = min(values)
        maximum = max(values)
        return minimum, maximum
    except ValueError:
        print(f"Error: Non-numeric data found in the column {column}.")
        return None

def write_summary_csv(file_path, average_age, salary_range):
    """
    Writes the average age and salary range to a new CSV file.
    Handles exceptions during file writing.
    """
    try:
        with open(file_path, 'w', newline='') as csvfile:
            fieldnames = ['Average Age', 'Salary Range (min-max)']
            writer = csv.DictWriter(csvfile, fieldnames=fieldnames)
            writer.writeheader()
            writer.writerow({
                'Average Age': average_age,
                'Salary Range (min-max)': f"{salary_range[0]}-{salary_range[1]}"
            })
        print("Summary written to summary.csv")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"An error occurred while writing to the file {file_path}: {e}")

def main():
    data = read_csv('data.csv')
    if data:
        average_age = calculate_average(data, 'Age')
        salary_range = find_min_max(data, 'Salary')
        if average_age is not None and salary_range is not None:
            write_summary_csv('summary.csv', average_age, salary_range)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Book` class to manage books in a library system. The class should encapsulate the following attributes:
- `title`: A string representing the title of the book.
- `author`: A string representing the author of the book.
- `isbn`: A string representing the International Standard Book Number of the book.
- `published_year`: An integer representing the year the book was published.
- `available`: A boolean indicating whether the book is available for checkout, defaulting to `True`.

Your class should provide the following methods:
- `checkout`: A method that sets the `available` attribute to `False`.
- `return_book`: A method that sets the `available` attribute to `True`.
- `__str__`: A method that returns a string representation of the book in the format: `"Title: {title}, Author: {author}, ISBN: {isbn}, Published Year: {published_year}, Available: {available}"`.

**Sample Input/Output:**
```python
book = Book("1984", "George Orwell", "9780451524935", 1949)
print(book)  # Output: Title: 1984, Author: George Orwell, ISBN: 9780451524935, Published Year: 1949, Available: True
book.checkout()
print(book)  # Output: Title: 1984, Author: George Orwell, ISBN: 9780451524935, Published Year: 1949, Available: False
book.return_book()
print(book)  # Output: Title: 1984, Author: George Orwell, ISBN: 9780451524935, Published Year: 1949, Available: True
```

```python
class Book:
    def __init__(self, title, author, isbn, published_year, available=True):
        """
        Initializes a new Book instance.

        :param title: The title of the book.
        :param author: The author of the book.
        :param isbn: The International Standard Book Number of the book.
        :param published_year: The year the book was published.
        :param available: A boolean indicating whether the book is available for checkout.
        """
        self.title = title
        self.author = author
        self.isbn = isbn
        self.published_year = published_year
        self.available = available

    def checkout(self):
        """
        Sets the book's availability to False, indicating it is checked out.
        """
        self.available = False

    def return_book(self):
        """
        Sets the book's availability to True, indicating it is returned.
        """
        self.available = True

    def __str__(self):
        """
        Returns a string representation of the book.

        :return: A string in the format "Title: {title}, Author: {author}, ISBN: {isbn}, Published Year: {published_year}, Available: {available}".
        """
        return (f"Title: {self.title}, Author: {self.author}, "
                f"ISBN: {self.isbn}, Published Year: {self.published_year}, "
                f"Available: {self.available}")
```<#sep#>You are tasked with designing a class hierarchy for managing employees and their salaries within a company. Implement a base class `Employee` with the following attributes: `employee_id`, `name`, `department`, and `salary`. The class should have an initializer method that accepts four parameters to set these attributes. Additionally, it should include a method `give_raise` that increases the `salary` by a specified percentage.

Next, create a subclass `Manager` that inherits from `Employee` and includes an additional attribute `team_size` to denote the number of employees managed by the manager. The `Manager` class should also have a method `add_employee_to_team` that increments the `team_size` by one.

**Sample Input/Output:**
- Input: 
  - Create an `Employee` object with `employee_id=1`, `name="John Doe"`, `department="Finance"`, `salary=50000`.
  - Call `give_raise(10)` on the `Employee` object.
  - Create a `Manager` object with `employee_id=2`, `name="Jane Smith"`, `department="IT"`, `salary=70000`, `team_size=5`.
  - Call `add_employee_to_team()` on the `Manager` object.
- Output:
  - The `Employee` object's salary should be 55000.
  - The `Manager` object's team size should be 6.

```python
class Employee:
    def __init__(self, employee_id, name, department, salary):
        """
        Initialize an Employee object with the given attributes.

        :param employee_id: Unique identifier for the employee.
        :param name: Name of the employee.
        :param department: Department where the employee works.
        :param salary: Salary of the employee.
        """
        self.employee_id = employee_id
        self.name = name
        self.department = department
        self.salary = salary

    def give_raise(self, percentage):
        """
        Increase the employee's salary by a specified percentage.

        :param percentage: The percentage by which to increase the salary.
        """
        self.salary += self.salary * (percentage / 100)

class Manager(Employee):
    def __init__(self, employee_id, name, department, salary, team_size=0):
        """
        Initialize a Manager object with the given attributes.

        :param employee_id: Unique identifier for the manager.
        :param name: Name of the manager.
        :param department: Department where the manager works.
        :param salary: Salary of the manager.
        :param team_size: Number of employees managed by the manager.
        """
        super().__init__(employee_id, name, department, salary)
        self.team_size = team_size

    def add_employee_to_team(self):
        """
        Increment the team size by one.
        """
        self.team_size += 1
```<#sep#>You are given a task to write a program that reads a list of integers from a specified input file, sorts them in ascending order, and writes the sorted list to a specified output file. Your program must handle command-line arguments for the input and output file paths using the `argparse` module. If the input file does not exist or is not readable, your program should raise a `FileNotFoundError`. If the output file cannot be written to, your program should raise a `PermissionError`.

**Input Specification:**
- The input file contains a single line of space-separated integers.

**Output Specification:**
- The output file should contain a single line of space-separated integers sorted in ascending order.

**Sample Input File (input.txt):**
```
3 1 4 1 5 9 2 6 5 3 5
```

**Sample Output File (output.txt):**
```
1 1 2 3 3 4 5 5 5 6 9
```

```python
import argparse

def read_integers(file_path):
    """
    Read integers from a given file path.
    
    Args:
        file_path (str): The path to the input file.
    
    Returns:
        list: A list of integers read from the file.
    
    Raises:
        FileNotFoundError: If the specified input file does not exist or is not readable.
    """
    try:
        with open(file_path, 'r') as file:
            return list(map(int, file.read().split()))
    except FileNotFoundError:
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"The specified input file '{file_path}' does not exist or is not readable.")

def write_integers(file_path, integers):
    """
    Write a list of integers to a given file path.
    
    Args:
        file_path (str): The path to the output file.
        integers (list): The list of integers to write to the file.
    
    Raises:
        PermissionError: If the specified output file cannot be written to.
    """
    try:
        with open(file_path, 'w') as file:
            file.write(' '.join(map(str, integers)))
    except PermissionError:
        raise PermissionError(f"The specified output file '{file_path}' cannot be written to.")

def main(input_file, output_file):
    """
    Read integers from the input file, sort them, and write to the output file.
    
    Args:
        input_file (str): The path to the input file.
        output_file (str): The path to the output file.
    """
    integers = read_integers(input_file)
    integers.sort()
    write_integers(output_file, integers)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description='Sort integers from input file and write to output file.')
    parser.add_argument('input_file', type=str, help='The path to the input file.')
    parser.add_argument('output_file', type=str, help='The path to the output file.')

    args = parser.parse_args()
    main(args.input_file, args.output_file)
