        self.name = new_name
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Database` class in Python that interacts with a SQLite database. The class should support the following operations:

1. **Connect**: Establish a connection to a SQLite database with a given name. If the database does not exist, it should be created.
2. **Add Record**: Insert a record into a specified table. The record is provided as a dictionary where keys are column names and values are the corresponding data to be inserted.
3. **Get Records**: Retrieve all records from a specified table. The method should return a list of dictionaries, each representing a record.
4. **Close**: Terminate the connection to the database.

**Sample Input/Output**:
- Assume a table `users` with columns `id`, `name`, and `age`.
- `connect("test.db")`
- `add_record("users", {"id": 1, "name": "Alice", "age": 30})`
- `add_record("users", {"id": 2, "name": "Bob", "age": 25})`
- `get_records("users")` should return `[{"id": 1, "name": "Alice", "age": 30}, {"id": 2, "name": "Bob", "age": 25}]`
- `close()`

```python
import sqlite3

class Database:
    def __init__(self):
        self.conn = None

    def connect(self, db_name):
        """
        Connects to a SQLite database with the name `db_name`.
        If the database does not exist, it will be created.
        """
        self.conn = sqlite3.connect(db_name)
        self.cursor = self.conn.cursor()

    def add_record(self, table_name, record):
        """
        Adds a record to the table `table_name`.
        The `record` is a dictionary where keys are column names and values are the values to be inserted.
        """
        if not self.conn:
            raise Exception("Database is not connected.")
        
        columns = ', '.join(record.keys())
        placeholders = ', '.join('?' * len(record))
        query = f"INSERT INTO {table_name} ({columns}) VALUES ({placeholders})"
        self.cursor.execute(query, tuple(record.values()))
        self.conn.commit()

    def get_records(self, table_name):
        """
        Retrieves all records from the table `table_name`.
        Returns a list of dictionaries, where each dictionary represents a record.
        """
        if not self.conn:
            raise Exception("Database is not connected.")
        
        self.cursor.execute(f"SELECT * FROM {table_name}")
        rows = self.cursor.fetchall()
        columns = [description[0] for description in self.cursor.description]
        return [dict(zip(columns, row)) for row in rows]

    def close(self):
        """
        Closes the connection to the database.
        """
        if self.conn:
            self.conn.close()
            self.conn = None
```<#sep#>You are tasked with developing a RESTful API using the Flask framework that interacts with a SQLite database. The API should support the following operations on a `users` table with columns `id`, `name`, `email`, and `age`:

1. **Retrieve all users** - Return a list of all users in the database.
2. **Retrieve a specific user** - Return details of a user identified by their `id`.
3. **Create a new user** - Add a new user to the database with the provided `name`, `email`, and `age`.
4. **Update an existing user** - Modify the details of an existing user identified by their `id`.
5. **Delete a user** - Remove a user from the database identified by their `id`.

The API should handle errors gracefully, returning appropriate HTTP status codes and messages. Assume that the Flask framework and SQLite library are already installed.

**Sample Requests and Responses:**

1. **Retrieve all users**
   - **Request:** `GET /users`
   - **Response:** `200 OK`
     ```json
     [
       {"id": 1, "name": "John Doe", "email": "john@example.com", "age": 30},
       {"id": 2, "name": "Jane Smith", "email": "jane@example.com", "age": 25}
     ]
     ```

2. **Retrieve a specific user**
   - **Request:** `GET /users/1`
   - **Response:** `200 OK`
     ```json
     {"id": 1, "name": "John Doe", "email": "john@example.com", "age": 30}
     ```

3. **Create a new user**
   - **Request:** `POST /users`
     ```json
     {"name": "Alice Johnson", "email": "alice@example.com", "age": 28}
     ```
   - **Response:** `201 Created`
     ```json
     {"message": "User created successfully"}
     ```

4. **Update an existing user**
   - **Request:** `PUT /users/1`
     ```json
     {"name": "Johnathan Doe", "email": "johnathan@example.com", "age": 31}
     ```
   - **Response:** `200 OK`
     ```json
     {"message": "User updated successfully"}
     ```

5. **Delete a user**
   - **Request:** `DELETE /users/1`
   - **Response:** `204 No Content`

```python
from flask import Flask, request, jsonify
import sqlite3
from sqlite3 import Error

app = Flask(__name__)
DATABASE = "users.db"

def init_db():
    """
    Initializes the SQLite database and creates the users table if it does not exist.
    """
    connection = None
    try:
        connection = sqlite3.connect(DATABASE)
        cursor = connection.cursor()
        cursor.execute("""
            CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS users (
                id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
                name TEXT NOT NULL,
                email TEXT NOT NULL,
                age INTEGER NOT NULL
            )
        """)
        connection.commit()
    except Error as e:
        print(e)
    finally:
        if connection:
            connection.close()

def get_db_connection():
    """
    Establishes a connection to the SQLite database and sets the row factory to sqlite3.Row.
    """
    conn = sqlite3.connect(DATABASE)
    conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    return conn

@app.route('/users', methods=['GET'])
def get_users():
    """
    Retrieves all users from the database.
    """
    conn = get_db_connection()
    users = conn.execute('SELECT * FROM users').fetchall()
    conn.close()
    return jsonify([dict(user) for user in users]), 200

@app.route('/users/<int:user_id>', methods=['GET'])
def get_user(user_id):
    """
    Retrieves a specific user by their ID.
    """
    conn = get_db_connection()
    user = conn.execute('SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = ?', (user_id,)).fetchone()
    conn.close()
    if user is None:
        return jsonify({"message": "User not found"}), 404
    return jsonify(dict(user)), 200

@app.route('/users', methods=['POST'])
def create_user():
    """
    Creates a new user in the database.
    """
    new_user = request.get_json()
    if not all(key in new_user for key in ('name', 'email', 'age')):
        return jsonify({"message": "Invalid request"}), 400
    conn = get_db_connection()
    try:
        conn.execute(
            'INSERT INTO users (name, email, age) VALUES (?, ?, ?)',
            (new_user['name'], new_user['email'], new_user['age'])
        )
        conn.commit()
        return jsonify({"message": "User created successfully"}), 201
    except Exception as e:
        return jsonify({"message": str(e)}), 400
    finally:
        conn.close()

@app.route('/users/<int:user_id>', methods=['PUT'])
def update_user(user_id):
    """
    Updates an existing user in the database.
    """
    update_data = request.get_json()
    if not all(key in update_data for key in ('name', 'email', 'age')):
        return jsonify({"message": "Invalid request"}), 400
    conn = get_db_connection()
    try:
        conn.execute(
            'UPDATE users SET name = ?, email = ?, age = ? WHERE id = ?',
            (update_data['name'], update_data['email'], update_data['age'], user_id)
        )
        conn.commit()
        if conn.total_changes == 0:
            return jsonify({"message": "User not found"}), 404
        return jsonify({"message": "User updated successfully"}), 200
    except Exception as e:
        return jsonify({"message": str(e)}), 400
    finally:
        conn.close()

@app.route('/users/<int:user_id>', methods=['DELETE'])
def delete_user(user_id):
    """
    Deletes a user from the database.
    """
    conn = get_db_connection()
    try:
        conn.execute('DELETE FROM users WHERE id = ?', (user_id,))
        conn.commit()
        if conn.total_changes == 0:
            return jsonify({"message": "User not found"}), 404
        return jsonify({"message": "User deleted successfully"}), 204
    except Exception as e:
        return jsonify({"message": str(e)}), 400
    finally:
        conn.close()

if __name__ == '__main__':
    init_db()
    app.run(debug=True)
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `BankAccount` class that simulates basic bank account operations. The class should support the following functionalities:

1. **Initialization**: The constructor should accept the account holder's name, account number, and an optional initial balance (default is 0).
2. **Deposit**: A method to deposit a specified amount into the account. The amount must be positive; otherwise, raise a `ValueError` with the message "Deposit amount must be positive".
3. **Withdraw**: A method to withdraw a specified amount from the account. The amount must be positive and should not exceed the current balance; otherwise, raise a `ValueError` with the message "Insufficient funds" or "Withdrawal amount must be positive".
4. **Check Balance**: A method to return the current balance of the account.
5. **Display Details**: A method to return a string containing the account holder's name, account number, and current balance.

**Sample Input/Output**:
- Input: `account = BankAccount("John Doe", "123456789", 1000)`
- Output: `account.display_account_details()` should return `"Account Holder: John Doe\nAccount Number: 123456789\nBalance: 1000"`
- Input: `account.deposit(500)`
- Output: `account.check_balance()` should return `1500`
- Input: `account.withdraw(200)`
- Output: `account.display_account_details()` should return `"Account Holder: John Doe\nAccount Number: 123456789\nBalance: 1300"`
- Input: `account.withdraw(1500)` should raise a `ValueError` with the message "Insufficient funds"

```python
class BankAccount:
    def __init__(self, account_holder_name, account_number, initial_balance=0):
        """
        Initialize a new bank account.

        :param account_holder_name: The name of the account holder.
        :param account_number: The account number.
        :param initial_balance: The initial balance of the account (default is 0).
        """
        self.account_holder_name = account_holder_name
        self.account_number = account_number
        self.balance = initial_balance

    def deposit(self, amount):
        """
        Deposit a specified amount into the account.

        :param amount: The amount to deposit.
        :raises ValueError: If the deposit amount is not positive.
        """
        if amount > 0:
            self.balance += amount
        else:
            raise ValueError("Deposit amount must be positive")

    def withdraw(self, amount):
        """
        Withdraw a specified amount from the account.

        :param amount: The amount to withdraw.
        :raises ValueError: If the withdrawal amount is not positive or exceeds the current balance.
        """
        if amount > self.balance:
            raise ValueError("Insufficient funds")
        elif amount <= 0:
            raise ValueError("Withdrawal amount must be positive")
        else:
            self.balance -= amount

    def check_balance(self):
        """
        Check the current balance of the account.

        :return: The current balance.
        """
        return self.balance

    def display_account_details(self):
        """
        Display the account details.

        :return: A string containing the account holder's name, account number, and current balance.
        """
        return f"Account Holder: {self.account_holder_name}\nAccount Number: {self.account_number}\nBalance: {self.balance}"
```<#sep#>You are given a dataset of transactions from an e-commerce platform. Each transaction is represented as a dictionary with the following keys:
- `user_id`: a unique identifier for the user.
- `product_id`: a unique identifier for the product.
- `quantity`: the number of units of the product purchased.
- `price`: the price per unit of the product.
- `timestamp`: the timestamp of the transaction.

Your task is to implement a function `calculate_total_revenue(transactions)` that computes the total revenue generated by each user. The function should return a dictionary where the keys are `user_id`s and the values are the total revenue generated by each user.

**Input:**
- A list of transactions, where each transaction is a dictionary with the keys `user_id`, `product_id`, `quantity`, `price`, and `timestamp`.

**Output:**
- A dictionary with `user_id`s as keys and the total revenue generated by each user as values.

**Sample Input:**
```python
transactions = [
    {"user_id": 1, "product_id": 101, "quantity": 2, "price": 29.99, "timestamp": "2021-01-15T14:23:01Z"},
    {"user_id": 1, "product_id": 102, "quantity": 1, "price": 15.50, "timestamp": "2021-01-15T15:45:23Z"},
    {"user_id": 2, "product_id": 101, "quantity": 3, "price": 29.99, "timestamp": "2021-01-15T17:00:00Z"}
]
```

**Sample Output:**
```python
{1: 75.48, 2: 89.97}
```

```python
def calculate_total_revenue(transactions):
    """
    Calculate the total revenue generated by each user from a list of transactions.

    :param transactions: List of dictionaries, each representing a transaction with keys:
                         'user_id', 'product_id', 'quantity', 'price', and 'timestamp'.
    :return: Dictionary with user_id as keys and total revenue as values.
    """
    total_revenue = {}
    
    for transaction in transactions:
        user_id = transaction['user_id']
        quantity = transaction['quantity']
        price = transaction['price']
        
        current_revenue = quantity * price
        
        if user_id in total_revenue:
            total_revenue[user_id] += current_revenue
        else:
            total_revenue[user_id] = current_revenue
    
    return total_revenue

# Example usage
transactions = [
    {"user_id": 1, "product_id": 101, "quantity": 2, "price": 29.99, "timestamp": "2021-01-15T14:23:01Z"},
    {"user_id": 1, "product_id": 102, "quantity": 1, "price": 15.50, "timestamp": "2021-01-15T15:45:23Z"},
    {"user_id": 2, "product_id": 101, "quantity": 3, "price": 29.99, "timestamp": "2021-01-15T17:00:00Z"}
]

print(calculate_total_revenue(transactions))
```<#sep#>You are tasked with developing a system to compute the total revenue generated from online course sales for a specific month. The system will receive a list of sales records, where each record includes the course ID, the sale date, the course price, and any applicable discount. Your function should return the total revenue for the specified month. Assume the input list is non-empty and all dates are formatted as `YYYY-MM-DD`.

**Input:**
- A list of tuples, where each tuple contains:
  - `course_id`: A string representing the course identifier.
  - `sale_date`: A string representing the date of the sale in the format `YYYY-MM-DD`.
  - `price`: A float representing the original price of the course.
  - `discount`: A float representing the discount percentage (e.g., 0.20 for a 20% discount).
- A string `target_month` representing the month for which to calculate the revenue in the format `YYYY-MM`.

**Output:**
- A float representing the total revenue generated in the specified month.

**Sample Input:**
```python
sales = [
    ('CS101', '2023-10-01', 79.99, 0.1),
    ('CS201', '2023-10-05', 129.99, 0.0),
    ('CS101', '2023-09-20', 79.99, 0.05),
    ('CS301', '2023-10-15', 99.99, 0.0),
    ('CS102', '2023-10-25', 89.99, 0.15),
]
target_month = '2023-10'
```

**Sample Output:**
```
404.91
```

```python
from datetime import datetime

def calculate_monthly_revenue(sales_records, target_month):
    """
    Calculate the total revenue generated from course sales for a given month.

    :param sales_records: List of sales records, where each record is a tuple (course_id, date, price, discount).
                          `date` is a string in the format 'YYYY-MM-DD'.
                          `price` is a float representing the original price of the course.
                          `discount` is a float representing the discount percentage (e.g., 0.20 for 20% discount).
    :param target_month: String representing the target month in the format 'YYYY-MM'.
    :return: Total revenue generated in the specified month.
    """
    total_revenue = 0.0

    for course_id, sale_date, price, discount in sales_records:
        sale_datetime = datetime.strptime(sale_date, '%Y-%m-%d')
        sale_month = sale_datetime.strftime('%Y-%m')
        
        if sale_month == target_month:
            final_price = price * (1 - discount)
            total_revenue += final_price

    return total_revenue

# Example usage:
sales = [
    ('CS101', '2023-10-01', 79.99, 0.1),
    ('CS201', '2023-10-05', 129.99, 0.0),
    ('CS101', '2023-09-20', 79.99, 0.05),
    ('CS301', '2023-10-15', 99.99, 0.0),
    ('CS102', '2023-10-25', 89.99, 0.15),
]

target_month = '2023-10'
print(calculate_monthly_revenue(sales, target_month))  # Output should be 404.91

```
Deposited $500.00. New balance is $1500.00.
Withdrew $200.00. New balance is $1300.00.
Insufficient funds.
Current balance is $1300.00.
Account Holder: John Smith
Account Number: 123456789
Balance: $1300.00
```

```python
class BankAccount:
    def __init__(self, account_holder_name, account_number, initial_balance=0.0):
        """
        Initialize a new bank account with the given account holder's name, account number, and initial balance.
        """
        self.account_holder_name = account_holder_name
        self.account_number = account_number
        self.balance = initial_balance

    def deposit(self, amount):
        """
        Deposit a specified amount into the account.
        """
        if amount > 0:
            self.balance += amount
            print(f"Deposited ${amount:.2f}. New balance is ${self.balance:.2f}.")
        else:
            print("Deposit amount must be positive.")

    def withdraw(self, amount):
        """
        Withdraw a specified amount from the account, ensuring the balance does not go below zero.
        """
        if amount > 0:
            if amount <= self.balance:
                self.balance -= amount
                print(f"Withdrew ${amount:.2f}. New balance is ${self.balance:.2f}.")
            else:
                print("Insufficient funds.")
        else:
            print("Withdrawal amount must be positive.")

    def display_balance(self):
        """
        Display the current balance of the account.
        """
        print(f"Current balance is ${self.balance:.2f}.")

    def display_account_info(self):
        """
        Display the account information, including the account holder's name, account number, and balance.
        """
        print(f"Account Holder: {self.account_holder_name}")
        print(f"Account Number: {self.account_number}")
        print(f"Balance: ${self.balance:.2f}")

# Example usage:
account = BankAccount("John Smith", "123456789", 1000.00)
account.display_account_info()
account.deposit(500)
account.withdraw(200)
account.withdraw(1500)
account.display_balance()
```<#sep#>You are given a CSV file named `employees.csv` containing data about employees. Each row in the file includes the following columns: `Employee ID`, `Name`, `Department`, and `Salary`. Your task is to write a Python program that calculates the total salary for each department and prints the results in the format "Department: Total Salary". The program should handle potential errors gracefully, such as missing files or incorrect data formats.

**Input:**
- A CSV file `employees.csv` with the following structure:
  ```
  Employee ID,Name,Department,Salary
  1,Alice,HR,50000
  2,Bob,Engineering,75000
  3,Charlie,HR,60000
  4,David,Engineering,80000
  5,Eve,Marketing,55000
  ```

**Output:**
- The program should output the total salary for each department:
  ```
  HR: 110000.0
  Engineering: 155000.0
  Marketing: 55000.0
  ```

**Constraints:**
- The CSV file may be missing or contain incorrect data formats. Your program should handle these cases gracefully.

```python
import csv

def calculate_total_salary_per_department(csv_file_path):
    """
    Calculate the total salary for each department from a given CSV file.

    Parameters:
    csv_file_path (str): The path to the CSV file containing employee data.

    Returns:
    None: Prints the total salary for each department.
    """
    department_salaries = {}
    
    try:
        with open(csv_file_path, mode='r', newline='') as csvfile:
            csv_reader = csv.DictReader(csvfile)
            
            required_columns = {'Employee ID', 'Name', 'Department', 'Salary'}
            if not required_columns.issubset(csv_reader.fieldnames):
                raise ValueError(f"The CSV file must contain the following columns: {required_columns}")
            
            for row in csv_reader:
                try:
                    department = row['Department']
                    salary = float(row['Salary'])
                    
                    if department not in department_salaries:
                        department_salaries[department] = 0
                    
                    department_salaries[department] += salary
                
                except ValueError:
                    print(f"Skipping row with invalid data: {row}")
    
    except FileNotFoundError:
        print(f"Error: The file {csv_file_path} was not found.")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"An error occurred: {str(e)}")
    
    for department, total_salary in department_salaries.items():
        print(f"{department}: {total_salary}")

# Example usage:
# calculate_total_salary_per_department("employees.csv")

```python
def add(x, y):
    """Return the sum of x and y."""
    return x + y

def subtract(x, y):
    """Return the difference of x and y."""
    return x - y

def multiply(x, y):
    """Return the product of x and y."""
    return x * y

def divide(x, y):
    """Return the quotient of x and y, or an error message if y is zero."""
    if y == 0:
        return "Error! Division by zero."
    return x / y

def calculator():
    """Run a simple calculator that performs basic arithmetic operations."""
    while True:
        print("\nOptions:")
        print("Enter 'add' to add two numbers")
        print("Enter 'subtract' to subtract two numbers")

            print("Goodbye!")
            break
        elif user_input in ('add', 'subtract', 'multiply', 'divide'):

                num1 = float(input("Enter first number: "))
                num2 = float(input("Enter second number: "))

                print("Invalid input! Please enter a number.")

                print(f"The result is {add(num1, num2)}")
            elif user_input == "subtract":
                print(f"The result is {subtract(num1, num2)}")
            elif user_input == "multiply":
                print(f"The result is {multiply(num1, num2)}")
            elif user_input == "divide":
                print(f"The result is {divide(num1, num2)}")
        else:
            print("Invalid Input!")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    calculator()
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a Python class named `FileAnalyzer` designed to analyze the contents of a text file. The class should support the following functionalities:

- **Initialization**: The constructor `__init__` should accept a single parameter `filename`, representing the path to the file to be analyzed.
- **File Reading**: The method `read_file` should read the entire content of the file and store it internally. Handle potential errors such as file not found or other exceptions gracefully.
- **Line Count**: The method `get_line_count` should return the total number of lines in the file.
- **Word Count**: The method `get_word_count` should return the total number of words in the file.
- **Character Count**: The method `get_char_count` should return the total number of characters in the file.

**Sample Input/Output**:
- Given a file `sample.txt` with the following content:
  ```
  Hello World
  This is a test.
  ```
- The expected outputs for the methods are:
  - `get_line_count()` should return `2`.
  - `get_word_count()` should return `6`.
  - `get_char_count()` should return `25` (including spaces and newline characters).

```python
class FileAnalyzer:
    def __init__(self, filename):
        """
        Initializes the FileAnalyzer object with the filename to be analyzed.
        
        :param filename: str, the path to the file to be analyzed.
        """
        self.filename = filename
        self.contents = None

    def read_file(self):
        """
        Reads the contents of the file and stores it in an internal variable.
        Handles file not found and other exceptions.
        """
        try:
            with open(self.filename, 'r', encoding='utf-8') as file:
                self.contents = file.read()
        except FileNotFoundError:
            print(f"Error: The file '{self.filename}' was not found.")
            self.contents = ''
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"An error occurred: {e}")
            self.contents = ''

    def get_line_count(self):
        """
        Returns the number of lines in the file.
        
        :return: int, the number of lines in the file.
        """
        if self.contents is None:
            print("Error: File contents are not read. Call read_file() first.")
            return 0
        return self.contents.count('\n') + 1 if self.contents else 0

    def get_word_count(self):
        """
        Returns the number of words in the file.
        
        :return: int, the number of words in the file.
        """
        if self.contents is None:
            print("Error: File contents are not read. Call read_file() first.")
            return 0
        return len(self.contents.split()) if self.contents else 0

    def get_char_count(self):
        """
        Returns the number of characters in the file.
        
        :return: int, the number of characters in the file.
        """
        if self.contents is None:
            print("Error: File contents are not read. Call read_file() first.")
            return 0
        return len(self.contents) if self.contents else 0
```<#sep#>You are given a CSV file named `products.csv` containing a list of products with their respective prices and quantities. Your task is to write a Python program that performs the following operations:

1. Calculate the total value of all products (price * quantity).
2. Identify the product with the highest price.
3. Identify the product with the lowest price.

The CSV file has the following format:
- The first row contains the headers: `product_name`, `price`, `quantity`.
- Each subsequent row contains the data for a single product.

**Input:**
- A CSV file named `products.csv` with the following content:
```
product_name,price,quantity
Laptop,1200.00,3
Smartphone,800.00,5
Tablet,300.00,10
```

**Output:**
- The program should output the total value of all products, the name and price of the highest priced product, and the name and price of the lowest priced product in the following format:
```
Total value of all products: $6100.00
Highest priced product: Laptop at $1200.00
Lowest priced product: Tablet at $300.00
```

```python
import csv

def process_products(csv_file='products.csv'):
    """
    Reads a CSV file containing product data and calculates the total value of all products,
    identifies the product with the highest price, and identifies the product with the lowest price.

    Parameters:
    csv_file (str): The path to the CSV file containing product data.

    Returns:
    None: Outputs the results directly.
    """
    total_value = 0
    highest_price_product = {'product_name': None, 'price': float('-inf')}
    lowest_price_product = {'product_name': None, 'price': float('inf')}

    with open(csv_file, mode='r') as file:
        reader = csv.DictReader(file)
        for row in reader:
            price = float(row['price'])
            quantity = int(row['quantity'])
            product_value = price * quantity
            total_value += product_value

            if price > highest_price_product['price']:
                highest_price_product['product_name'] = row['product_name']
                highest_price_product['price'] = price

            if price < lowest_price_product['price']:
                lowest_price_product['product_name'] = row['product_name']
                lowest_price_product['price'] = price

    print(f"Total value of all products: ${total_value:.2f}")
    print(f"Highest priced product: {highest_price_product['product_name']} at ${highest_price_product['price']:.2f}")
    print(f"Lowest priced product: {lowest_price_product['product_name']} at ${lowest_price_product['price']:.2f}")

process_products()

- **Create Account**: Create a new account with a unique account number and an initial deposit. The initial deposit must be non-negative.
- **Deposit**: Add a specified amount to an existing account. The deposit amount must be positive.

- **Check Balance**: Retrieve the current balance of an existing account.

The system should handle invalid operations gracefully by printing appropriate error messages.

- The first line contains an integer `Q` representing the number of operations.
- Each of the next `Q` lines contains an operation in one of the following formats:
  - `CREATE <account_number> <initial_deposit>`
  - `DEPOSIT <account_number> <amount>`
  - `WITHDRAW <account_number> <amount>`
  - `CHECK <account_number>`

**Output Format:**
- For each operation, print the result or an error message as specified in the problem statement.

**Sample Input:**
```
7
CREATE A12345 1000

DEPOSIT A12345 200
DEPOSIT B54321 300
WITHDRAW A12345 150
WITHDRAW B54321 800
WITHDRAW B54321 100

Account B54321 created successfully with initial deposit of $500.
$200 deposited to account A12345. Current balance: $1200.
$300 deposited to account B54321. Current balance: $800.
$150 withdrawn from account A12345. Current balance: $1050.

```python
class SimpleBankingSystem:
    def __init__(self):
        self.accounts = {}

    def create_account(self, account_number, initial_deposit):
        """
        Create a new account with the given account number and initial deposit.
        """
        if account_number in self.accounts:
            print(f"Account {account_number} already exists.")
            return
        if initial_deposit < 0:
            print("Initial deposit cannot be negative.")
            return
        self.accounts[account_number] = initial_deposit
        print(f"Account {account_number} created successfully with initial deposit of ${initial_deposit}.")

    def deposit(self, account_number, amount):
        """
        Deposit a specified amount into the account with the given account number.
        """
        if account_number not in self.accounts:
            print(f"Account {account_number} does not exist.")
            return
        if amount <= 0:
            print("Deposit amount must be positive.")
            return
        self.accounts[account_number] += amount
        print(f"${amount} deposited to account {account_number}. Current balance: ${self.accounts[account_number]}.")

    def withdraw(self, account_number, amount):
        """
        Withdraw a specified amount from the account with the given account number.
        """
        if account_number not in self.accounts:
            print(f"Account {account_number} does not exist.")
            return
        if amount <= 0:
            print("Withdrawal amount must be positive.")
            return
        if amount > self.accounts[account_number]:
            print("Insufficient funds. You cannot withdraw more than the current balance.")
            return
        self.accounts[account_number] -= amount
        print(f"${amount} withdrawn from account {account_number}. Current balance: ${self.accounts[account_number]}.")

    def check_balance(self, account_number):
        """
        Check the balance of the account with the given account number.
        """
        if account_number not in self.accounts:
            print(f"Account {account_number} does not exist.")
            return
        print(f"Current balance of account {account_number}: ${self.accounts[account_number]}.")

# Example usage
bank_system = SimpleBankingSystem()

# Read input
import sys
input = sys.stdin.read
data = input().split()

Q = int(data[0])
index = 1

while index < len(data):
    operation = data[index]
    if operation == "CREATE":
        account_number = data[index + 1]
        initial_deposit = int(data[index + 2])
        bank_system.create_account(account_number, initial_deposit)
        index += 3
    elif operation == "DEPOSIT":
        account_number = data[index + 1]
        amount = int(data[index + 2])
        bank_system.deposit(account_number, amount)
        index += 3
    elif operation == "WITHDRAW":
        account_number = data[index + 1]
        amount = int(data[index + 2])
        bank_system.withdraw(account_number, amount)
        index += 3
    elif operation == "CHECK":
        account_number = data[index + 1]
        bank_system.check_balance(account_number)
        index += 2
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a simple stock market simulation in Python. Your program should define a class `StockMarket` with the following functionalities:

- **Initialization**: The constructor `__init__` should accept a dictionary where keys are stock symbols (strings) and values are their prices (floats).
- **Price Update**: The method `update_price` should update the price of a specified stock symbol. If the symbol does not exist, it should raise a `KeyError`.
- **Price Retrieval**: The method `get_price` should return the current price of a specified stock symbol. If the symbol does not exist, it should raise a `KeyError`.
- **Highest Price**: The method `get_highest_price` should return a tuple containing the stock symbol and its price that has the highest price. If no stocks are present, it should return `None`.
- **Lowest Price**: The method `get_lowest_price` should return a tuple containing the stock symbol and its price that has the lowest price. If no stocks are present, it should return `None`.

**Sample Input/Output**:
- Initialization: `StockMarket({'AAPL': 150, 'GOOGL': 2800, 'MSFT': 300})`
- `get_price('AAPL')` should return `150`
- `get_highest_price()` should return `('GOOGL', 2800)`
- `get_lowest_price()` should return `('AAPL', 150)`
- After `update_price('AAPL', 160)`, `get_price('AAPL')` should return `160`
- After the update, `get_highest_price()` should return `('GOOGL', 2800)`
- After the update, `get_lowest_price()` should return `('MSFT', 300)`

```python
class StockMarket:
    def __init__(self, stocks):
        """
        Initializes the stock market with a dictionary of stock symbols and their prices.
        
        :param stocks: A dictionary where keys are stock symbols and values are their prices.
        """
        self.stocks = stocks

    def update_price(self, symbol, new_price):
        """
        Updates the price of a given stock symbol.
        
        :param symbol: The stock symbol to update.
        :param new_price: The new price for the stock.
        :raises KeyError: If the stock symbol does not exist in the market.
        """
        if symbol in self.stocks:
            self.stocks[symbol] = new_price
        else:
            raise KeyError(f"Stock symbol '{symbol}' not found in the market.")

    def get_price(self, symbol):
        """
        Returns the current price of a given stock symbol.
        
        :param symbol: The stock symbol to retrieve the price for.
        :return: The current price of the stock.
        :raises KeyError: If the stock symbol does not exist in the market.
        """
        if symbol in self.stocks:
            return self.stocks[symbol]
        else:
            raise KeyError(f"Stock symbol '{symbol}' not found in the market.")

    def get_highest_price(self):
        """
        Returns the symbol and price of the stock with the highest price.
        
        :return: A tuple containing the stock symbol and its price, or None if no stocks are present.
        """
        if not self.stocks:
            return None
        highest_symbol = max(self.stocks, key=self.stocks.get)
        return highest_symbol, self.stocks[highest_symbol]

    def get_lowest_price(self):
        """
        Returns the symbol and price of the stock with the lowest price.
        
        :return: A tuple containing the stock symbol and its price, or None if no stocks are present.
        """
        if not self.stocks:
            return None
        lowest_symbol = min(self.stocks, key=self.stocks.get)
        return lowest_symbol, self.stocks[lowest_symbol]

# Example usage
stock_market = StockMarket({'AAPL': 150, 'GOOGL': 2800, 'MSFT': 300})

print(stock_market.get_price('AAPL'))  # Output: 150
print(stock_market.get_highest_price())  # Output: ('GOOGL', 2800)
print(stock_market.get_lowest_price())  # Output: ('AAPL', 150)

stock_market.update_price('AAPL', 160)
print(stock_market.get_price('AAPL'))  # Output: 160
print(stock_market.get_highest_price())  # Output: ('GOOGL', 2800)
print(stock_market.get_lowest_price())  # Output: ('MSFT', 300)
```<#sep#>You are given a list of `n` points in a 2D plane, where each point is represented as a tuple `(x, y)`. Your task is to find the pair of points that are closest to each other in terms of Euclidean distance. Implement a function `find_minimal_distance_pair(points)` that returns a tuple containing the coordinates of the two closest points. Your solution should have a time complexity better than O(N^2).

**Constraints:**
- 2 ≤ n ≤ 10^5
- -10^9 ≤ x, y ≤ 10^9

**Sample Input:**
```python
points = [(2, 3), (12, 30), (40, 50), (5, 1), (12, 10), (3, 4)]
```

**Sample Output:**
```python
((2, 3), (3, 4))
```

```python
import math

def distance(p1, p2):
    """Calculate the Euclidean distance between two points p1 and p2."""
    return math.sqrt((p1[0] - p2[0]) ** 2 + (p1[1] - p2[1]) ** 2)

def closest_pair_naive(points):
    """Find the closest pair of points using a naive O(N^2) approach."""
    min_dist = float('inf')
    pair = None
    n = len(points)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            d = distance(points[i], points[j])
            if d < min_dist:
                min_dist = d
                pair = (points[i], points[j])
    return pair, min_dist

def closest_split_pair(strip, d, closest_pair):
    """Find the closest pair of points that lie across the dividing line."""
    min_dist = d
    strip = sorted(strip, key=lambda point: point[1])
    n = len(strip)
    for i in range(n):
        j = i + 1
        while j < n and strip[j][1] - strip[i][1] < min_dist:
            d = distance(strip[i], strip[j])
            if d < min_dist:
                min_dist = d
                closest_pair = strip[i], strip[j]
            j += 1
    return closest_pair, min_dist

def closest_pair_rec(px, py):
    """Recursively find the closest pair of points using a divide and conquer approach."""
    if len(px) <= 3:
        return closest_pair_naive(px)
    mid = len(px) // 2
    Qx = px[:mid]
    Rx = px[mid:]
    midpoint = px[mid][0]
    Qy = list(filter(lambda x: x[0] <= midpoint, py))
    Ry = list(filter(lambda x: x[0] > midpoint, py))
    (p1, q1), dist1 = closest_pair_rec(Qx, Qy)
    (p2, q2), dist2 = closest_pair_rec(Rx, Ry)
    delta = min(dist1, dist2)
    closest_pair = (p1, q1) if dist1 <= dist2 else (p2, q2)
    strip = [point for point in py if abs(point[0] - midpoint) < delta]
    closest_split, dist_split = closest_split_pair(strip, delta, closest_pair)
    return closest_split, dist_split if dist_split < delta else closest_pair, delta

def find_minimal_distance_pair(points):
    """Find the pair of points with the minimal distance between them."""
    px = sorted(points, key=lambda x: x[0])
    py = sorted(points, key=lambda x: x[1])
    (p1, p2), _ = closest_pair_rec(px, py)
    return (p1, p2)
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a Python class `CSVFileHandler` that provides functionalities to read, write, and append data to CSV files. The class should handle exceptions such as file not found errors and invalid CSV formats gracefully. The `read_csv` method should return a list of dictionaries, where each dictionary represents a row in the CSV file. The `write_csv` method should overwrite the existing file with new data, and the `append_to_csv` method should add new data to the end of the file. Your implementation should include appropriate comments and docstrings.

**Sample Input:**
```
# File: example.csv
name,age
John,30
Jane,25

# Operations:
handler = CSVFileHandler()
data = handler.read_csv('example.csv')
handler.write_csv('example.csv', [{'name': 'Alice', 'age': 28}, {'name': 'Bob', 'age': 24}])
handler.append_to_csv('example.csv', [{'name': 'Charlie', 'age': 22}])
```

**Sample Output:**
```
# File: example.csv after operations
name,age
Alice,28
Bob,24
Charlie,22
```

```python
import csv
from typing import List, Dict


class CSVFileHandler:
    """A class to handle reading, writing, and appending to CSV files."""

    def read_csv(self, file_path: str) -> List[Dict]:
        """
        Reads a CSV file and returns a list of dictionaries where each
        dictionary represents a row in the CSV file.

        :param file_path: Path to the CSV file.
        :return: List of dictionaries where each dictionary is a row.
        :raises FileNotFoundError: If the file is not found.
        :raises csv.Error: If the CSV file has an invalid format.
        """
        try:
            with open(file_path, mode='r', newline='', encoding='utf-8') as file:
                reader = csv.DictReader(file)
                return list(reader)
        except FileNotFoundError:
            print(f"Error: The file at {file_path} was not found.")
            raise
        except csv.Error as e:
            print(f"Error reading CSV file at {file_path}: {e}")
            raise

    def write_csv(self, file_path: str, data: List[Dict]):
        """
        Writes a list of dictionaries to a CSV file, overwriting any existing data.

        :param file_path: Path to the CSV file.
        :param data: List of dictionaries to write to the file.
        :raises csv.Error: If the CSV file cannot be written properly.
        """
        if not data:
            print("Warning: No data to write to the CSV file.")
            return

        try:
            fieldnames = data[0].keys()
            with open(file_path, mode='w', newline='', encoding='utf-8') as file:
                writer = csv.DictWriter(file, fieldnames=fieldnames)
                writer.writeheader()
                writer.writerows(data)
        except csv.Error as e:
            print(f"Error writing to CSV file at {file_path}: {e}")
            raise
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Unexpected error occurred: {e}")
            raise

    def append_to_csv(self, file_path: str, data: List[Dict]):
        """
        Appends a list of dictionaries to a CSV file.

        :param file_path: Path to the CSV file.
        :param data: List of dictionaries to append to the file.
        :raises csv.Error: If the CSV file cannot be appended properly.
        """
        if not data:
            print("Warning: No data to append to the CSV file.")
            return

        try:
            if not self._file_exists(file_path):
                raise FileNotFoundError

            fieldnames = data[0].keys()
            with open(file_path, mode='a', newline='', encoding='utf-8') as file:
                writer = csv.DictWriter(file, fieldnames=fieldnames)
                writer.writerows(data)
        except FileNotFoundError:
            print(f"Error: The file at {file_path} was not found.")
            raise
        except csv.Error as e:
            print(f"Error appending to CSV file at {file_path}: {e}")
            raise
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Unexpected error occurred: {e}")
            raise

    @staticmethod
    def _file_exists(file_path: str) -> bool:
        """
        Checks if a file exists at the given path.

        :param file_path: Path to the file.
        :return: True if the file exists, False otherwise.
        """
        try:
            with open(file_path, mode='r', encoding='utf-8'):
                return True
        except FileNotFoundError:
            return False
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a library management system. Design a class `Book` with the following attributes and methods:

- **Attributes:**
  - `title`: A string representing the book's title.
  - `author`: A string representing the author's name.
  - `ISBN`: A string representing the book's ISBN number.
  - `available`: A boolean indicating whether the book is available in the library.

- **Methods:**
  - `borrow_book()`: Marks the book as borrowed by setting `available` to `False`.
  - `return_book()`: Marks the book as returned by setting `available` to `True`.
  - `get_book_info()`: Returns a string containing the book's title, author, and ISBN number.

Additionally, create a class `Library` that can hold a collection of books. It should have the following methods:

- **Methods:**
  - `add_book(book)`: Adds a new book to the library.
  - `find_book(title)`: Searches for a book by its title and returns it if found. If not found, it should return `None`.
  - `list_available_books()`: Returns a list of strings, each containing the title, author, and ISBN number of all available books in the library.

**Sample Input/Output:**

```python
# Create books
book1 = Book("1984", "George Orwell", "9780451524935")
book2 = Book("To Kill a Mockingbird", "Harper Lee", "9780060935467")

# Create library and add books
library = Library()
library.add_book(book1)
library.add_book(book2)

# Borrow a book
book1.borrow_book()

# List available books
print(library.list_available_books())  # Output: ['Title: To Kill a Mockingbird, Author: Harper Lee, ISBN: 9780060935467']

# Find a book
found_book = library.find_book("1984")
print(found_book.get_book_info())  # Output: Title: 1984, Author: George Orwell, ISBN: 9780451524935

# Return a book
book1.return_book()

# List available books again
print(library.list_available_books())  # Output: ['Title: 1984, Author: George Orwell, ISBN: 9780451524935', 'Title: To Kill a Mockingbird, Author: Harper Lee, ISBN: 9780060935467']
```

```python
class Book:
    def __init__(self, title, author, ISBN, available=True):
        """
        Initializes a new book with the given title, author, ISBN, and availability status.
        """
        self.title = title
        self.author = author
        self.ISBN = ISBN
        self.available = available

    def borrow_book(self):
        """
        Marks the book as borrowed by setting its availability to False.
        """
        if self.available:
            self.available = False

    def return_book(self):
        """
        Marks the book as returned by setting its availability to True.
        """
        self.available = True

    def get_book_info(self):
        """
        Returns a string containing the book's title, author, and ISBN number.
        """
        return f"Title: {self.title}, Author: {self.author}, ISBN: {self.ISBN}"

class Library:
    def __init__(self):
        """
        Initializes a new library with an empty collection of books.
        """
        self.books = []

    def add_book(self, book):
        """
        Adds a new book to the library.
        """
        self.books.append(book)

    def find_book(self, title):
        """
        Searches for a book by its title and returns it if found. If not found, returns None.
        """
        for book in self.books:
            if book.title == title:
                return book
        return None

    def list_available_books(self):
        """
        Returns a list of strings, each containing the title, author, and ISBN number of all available books in the library.
        """
        return [book.get_book_info() for book in self.books if book.available]

def find_developer_by_language(developers, language):
    """

    :param developers: Dictionary of developers with their personal information and skills.
    :param language: The programming language to search for.
    :return: List of developer names proficient in the specified language.
